- For each player j the partner gives, their perceived value is v′_j = max(v_j, W) × (1 + e_j) (the floor since Phase 4b, Section 9), e_j = e_rank + e_elite:
  - **e_rank** by j's rank by market value on the partner's roster (ties: more ROS points): #1 → 0.10, #2 → 0.05, others → 0. By market value, not lineup cost: managers think of "my best players" by name and value.
  - **e_elite** = 0.10 if j is among the top 12 overall by FantasyCalc value in the league's format.
  - **Dynasty only:** for a rebuilding partner (P(playoffs) below 25%), e is halved for RBs and WRs aged 27 or older.
  - No separate contender multiplier: contenders already weigh lineup gains more (n × 1.3).
- **Where it's used:** v′ replaces v for the partner's given players in the partner's g (quick and full acceptance). The waiver credit is unchanged. My g_mkt and B use values without the premium (floored at W like every package since Phase 4b).
- **Calibration:** these are starting assumptions. Phase 6 fits a scale factor for e alongside the β refit, from Accepted and Rejected outcomes and "They'd never accept" reports.
- **Card reason** when it applies ("Their best player (or a top-12 player): expect them to want a little extra."): see Section 13.
- Phase 4 readings (`src/engine/premium.ts`):
  - The rank counts every player the partner rosters (active, IR and taxi), by perception value (estimates and floors count; a player without a model counts 0), ties by more ROS points, then the smaller id. The top 12 is the whole FantasyCalc perception list for the format (FantasyCalc values only; ties at the cut by the smaller id). The trade context uses its own baseline's P(playoffs) for the dynasty halving; the League page uses the League model's odds.
  - ⚠️ Everything except the acceptance g stays without the premium (floored at W like every package since Phase 4b): TradeEvaluation.gPartner (the reasons' clauses and the report, and the Trade Analyzer's "Details" until Phase 7b; no card prints it since Phase 4c, no page since Phase 7b), hist (past trades), the §12 screening window, and the veto rule (league members judge a trade by the market, not by the owner's attachment).
  - v′ is applied before the stud exponent (identical at γ = 1).
- **Anchor tests** (default coefficients, W = 752, within ±0.5 points; through the real package and acceptance functions; Phase 4b's numbers, after the β_need change, Checkpoint 4's in brackets):

  | Case | P_accept |
  |---|---|
  | 1-for-1, equal raw value; partner's #1; top-12; contender; no lineup change | 10.9% |
  | 1-for-1, equal raw value; partner's #6; not top-12 | 35.4% (unchanged) |
  | Partner gets 4,000 + 3,500 + 3,500 for their #1 top-12 player (9,000); contender; n = 0.6; fills a hole | 32.8% (23.4%; 51.3% before the addendum) |
  | The same trade, where the RBs help them twice as much (n = 1.2) | 55.4% (32.8%) |
  | 1-for-1, equal raw value; partner's #2; not top-12 | 26.3% |
  | 1-for-1; partner's #1, not top-12; contender; partner gets 30% more raw value | 62.0% |

**"Harder to get"** (addendum §2): a player with e ≥ 0.15 for his owner (a top-12 player who is also his team's #1 or #2) is tagged on the League page and on trade cards (Sections 13 and 14). In Parker's league: every team's #1, plus two #2s (12 players).

**Phase 4 readings** (`src/engine/acceptance.ts`):
- **n:** the blend weight is config (`nBlendWeight` 0.5 on the next 4 weeks), from the first week team numbers count, every remaining week unweighted (regular season and playoffs).
- **h:** a hole is one of P's slot instances whose starter averages below 60% of the league median there over the next 3 weeks (P's V* lineups). The trade fills it when that instance's starter averages more after the trade and a received player who can play the slot starts for P. Instances are sorted by points within a slot, so a received RB better than P's RB1 fills an RB2 hole by pushing the old RB1 down (review of track B). A hole only a free agent fills doesn't count. ⚠️ A cascade through two different flex slots (a TE at W/T pushing a WR into a W/R hole) is missed.
- **c** is never below 0 (a 1-for-0 the Analyzer can build isn't simpler than a 1-for-1).
- **a:** "the last N weeks" are the transactions whose week is above (league week, else NFL week) − N; any transaction type counts; trades count only once completed.
- **hist:** the average of P's completed trades' g at current perception values; only the average is clamped (review of track B). A trade where P gave nothing of value counts +∞, so hist is +0.15. Left out: trades without both of P's sides (hidden, or refilled ESPN trades whose players were dropped since), with a player who has no current value, or with picks (until picks have values).
- **hot:** the last 2 completed NFL weeks (not his last 2 games), against Sleeper's past projection, else ESPN's; his points are Sleeper's stat lines scored with the league's rules (the model inputs' actual points), so a player without a Sleeper id is never hot. ⚠️ Sleeper re-stamps past projections after the games (Section 2.3).
- **urg:** from 7 days before the deadline date through the deadline; with only a week, the deadline week and the week before; 0 after.
- **Display:** rounded to the nearest 5%, and the label is judged on the shown value (14.9% shows "15%" and reads "Possible"). Phase 4c (planner reply to Checkpoint 4b, answer 5): a value under 5% shows as "<5%", never rounded up to 5% (before, the clamp's 2% showed as 5%).

**Context (ctx).** P(playoffs) here always comes from the baseline simulation (before any trade), computed once per search.
- **Redraft and keeper:** if P's P(playoffs) is below 5%, −0.8 (they've checked out).
- **Dynasty, rebuilding partner** (P(playoffs) below 25%): −0.25 for each RB or WR they'd receive who is 28 or older (the ages live in config).
- **Any league, contender partner** (P(playoffs) above 60%): multiply n by 1.3.

**Recency (hot)**
- +0.15 if a player P receives scored at least 1.5× his projection in each of the last 2 weeks.
- −0.15 if a player P gives up did that.
- Past-week projections come from Sleeper's projections endpoint for that past week, or from ESPN statSourceId 1 entries for that scoring period. Actual points come from the league's matchup data. If either is missing, hot = 0.

### Model

```
z = β0 + β_gain·g⁺ + β_loss·g⁻ + β_need·n + β_hole·h − β_cx·c + β_act·a − β_hist·hist + ctx + hot + urg
P_accept = clamp(σ(z), 0.02, 0.85)          // σ = logistic function (the cap was 0.95 before Phase 4b)
```

Default coefficients:

| β0 | β_gain | β_loss | β_need | β_hole | β_cx | β_act | β_hist |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| −0.6 | 6 | 9 | 1.2 (redraft, keeper) / 0.6 (dynasty) | 0.4 | 0.25 | 0.35 | 3 |

β_loss is larger than β_gain on purpose: people feel losses more than gains.

**Phase 4b** (planner reply to Checkpoint 4, change 2): β_need is 1.2 in redraft and keeper leagues (`beta.need`): there a manager's own lineup should weigh more (Checkpoint 4's top card showed 55% for a trade that cost the partner's lineup 4.7 points a week). Dynasty stays 0.6 (`beta.needDynasty`). The contender factor's extra term uses the same β_need. The cap is 0.85: no offer is a sure thing, and Overall still never exceeds Helps you.

**Veto risk**
- If `tradeReview` ≠ 'none' and g < −0.30: `P_complete = P_accept × 0.5`.
- Otherwise `P_complete = P_accept`.
- Rank on `P_complete`, and show both numbers when they differ.

**Required anchor tests**

With the default coefficients, unit tests must hit these values within ±0.5 points (Phase 4b; ±1 before). The planner computed them (redraft unless noted; Checkpoint 4's numbers in brackets where they changed):

| Scenario | P_accept |
|---|---|
| Even value; n = 0.5; fills a hole; active (a = +1) | 67.9% (61.1%) |
| Even value; nothing else | 35.4% |
| g = −0.10; n = 0.5; fills a hole | 37.8% (31.0%) |
| g = −0.20; nothing else | 8.3% |
| g = +0.15; n = −0.3 | 48.5% (53.0%) |
| Even value; inactive (a = −2) | 21.4% |
| 3-for-1 (c = 2); even value | 25.0% |
| g = +0.30; n = 0.5 | 85.0%, the cap (81.8%) |
| Dynasty: even value; n = 0.5; fills a hole; active | 61.1% |

**Labels**

| P_accept | Label |
|---|---|
| under 15% | Long shot |
| 15–35% | Possible |
| 35–55% | Decent |
| 55% and up | Likely |

**Feedback on every suggestion**

The model will be wrong in places, especially the acceptance estimate until it has real results to learn from.
- **On each trade card:**
  - outcome buttons: **Sent**, **Accepted**, **Rejected**, **Countered**
  - a **Report** button with these reasons: Doesn't help me · They'd never accept · Player info is wrong (injured, traded, wrong position) · League settings look wrong · Other, with an optional note
- **Phase 4 (local only):** store each report and outcome in the browser, together with the trade's model inputs and scores. Settings has an "Export feedback" button that downloads them as JSON. A script summarizes reports by reason and by model part.
- **Phase 4b** (Checkpoint 4 answer 8): the record also keeps which version was sent: "Offer first", "Full trade" or "Plan B". A card that shows two versions asks which one on the first outcome pressed, worded for that outcome ("Which version did you send?", "… did they accept?", "… did they turn down?", "… did they counter?"); a plain card records the full trade; clearing the outcome clears the version, and the card's "Version:" line says "an earlier one (…)" when the version recorded is no longer one the card shows. The record keeps the sent version's trade key and estimate, and since Phase 4c (planner reply to Checkpoint 4b, change 7 and answer 9) its model inputs too (`versionInputs`: the acceptance features g, n, h, c, a, hist, ctx, hot, urg and z, where g is the partner's perceived g with the star premium; the partner's g without the premium, `gPartner`, so the premium is the difference; B; and the goal B was weighted by), so Phase 6 can refit on the version actually sent (from the anonymous copies: the export carried the inputs until Checkpoint 7d, without their value ratios from the planner's reply to Checkpoint 7b, Q5, and since then holds what the user saw and entered: below), `npm run feedback:summary` counts outcomes by version, and older records read as unknown. Phase 6 analyzes it.
- **The export file holds only what the user saw and entered** (Checkpoint 7d; planner reply to Checkpoint 7c, answer 1: "export only what the user saw and entered: the trade; the numbers on its card: Helps you, Helps them, the chance they accept, Overall and the odds lines; the outcome, the version sent and the notes. Leave out everything else the model used, so no value ratio can be worked back out. The anonymous server copies keep everything, as before."; `src/lib/feedbackStore.ts` feedbackExport, `FEEDBACK_EXPORT_VERSION` 3): each exported record holds the league as the file numbers it (league-1, …), the UTC day the record was made (`madeOn`, its first press) and those of its latest outcome and its report (⚠️ ours: when the user acted, to the day; a record keeps no earlier outcome's time, so an outcome pressed before the latest has no day in the file and the Settings page's words never say "each press": review of track cards), the trade (the partner's team id and the players given and received, as the record holds them), what its card printed (`shown`: Helps you, the chance they accept with its label and, when the league's review could veto it and that printed differently, the chance it goes through, Overall, my odds lines and weekly line, and Helps them with their odds when the card showed the partner's line, each the card's own text), the outcome, the version sent (which one, its players read from its key, and its chance as its line printed it, from the estimate the record keeps for it), the report's reason and the note. Around the records the file says what it is, how many it holds and the UTC day it was exported (`exportedAt`, the day its name gives; ⚠️ ours, review of Checkpoint 7d, cards-export-1: until then its time to the millisecond, the one time of day in the file). Never B, B₃, the raw P_accept or P_complete, g, the acceptance features, ΔPO, ΔCH, ΔPPW, the basis, the version's inputs, the share id, a trade key or a record id (⚠️ ours: made from the trade key and the first press's time, an id would give that time back). Where the printed numbers come from: each press on a card carries them (`FeedbackChange.shown`, the card's `tradeView.ts` `TradeCardView.shown`, added by `TradeCard.tsx` through `cardPress`), and a new record keeps them (`FeedbackRecord.shown`, its first press's, like its numbers); `parseFeedbackShown` keeps only texts in the card's printed forms, at the card's own precision (the chance in whole steps of `config.acceptance.displayStep`, "<5%" under it, and the chance it goes through likewise; scores 0–100; the title odds to a tenth, every other odds value and the weekly line in whole percents, at most `config.explain.weeklyLineMax` weeks; review of Checkpoint 7d, cards-export-2: until then any whole percent passed for the chance, so a slip that printed the raw estimate to the percent would have ridden into the file), so no raw number can ride in them; the version's chance is held to the same forms. ⚠️ Ours: a record made before has none, so the export works its three numbers out from its own with the card's formatting (`src/lib/printed.ts`: the card's own `acceptText`, `throughText` and `printedScore`, moved there so the store prints without the view, which re-exports them; the label, the engine's `acceptanceLabel`), which prints exactly what its card did, and leaves its lines out. `feedbackStore.test.ts` proves it: an allowlist of the file's keys, no number in a record but a pick's season and round, and two records whose model numbers differ (g, the features, B, B₃, ΔPO, ΔCH, the raw estimates) but print the same exporting the same file byte for byte, with and without their cards' printed numbers. The record in the browser keeps everything, and the anonymous copy is built as before (`feedbackShare.test.ts` pins it byte for byte: the copy Checkpoint 7c's code builds for the same record). `parseFeedbackExport` reads versions 1 to 3 and a bare list of records, each as version 3 holds it (an older record's numbers printed as above), and `npm run feedback:summary` summarizes what version 3 holds: by outcome, by version sent (the card's own trade when the version's players are the card's), by reason with the averages of the printed numbers (Helps you, Helps them where shown, the chance, Overall), and the calibration on the printed chances (the version's line's when recorded, else the card's; ⚠️ ours: "<5%" counted as half of 5%). The Settings page says what the file holds (`FeedbackCard.tsx` `EXPORT_TEXT`; the problem reported named since the review of Checkpoint 7d, cards-export-1, as the feedback page names it): "The file holds what you saw and entered: each trade (its players and the other team as ID numbers), the numbers on its card, your outcome, the version you sent, the problem you reported and your notes, with the day you first marked or reported each trade and the days of its latest outcome and report. Apart from what your notes say, it has no names, and it numbers your leagues (league-1, league-2) rather than saying which they are." History: version 2 (planner reply to Checkpoint 7b, Q5) left out the value ratios' own fields (g_mkt for both sides, the perceived g, hist, the logit z) but kept B, the raw estimates and the features, which gave the ratios back to a reader who worked at it (the review of that change worked both routes: z from the raw P_accept less the features' terms gave the partner's perceived g, and B less B_win gave g_mkt(me) to anyone who knows κ); version 1 held every record as stored.
- **Phase 7 (public launch):**
  - Send reports and outcomes to a small server-side store on a free tier.
  - Store only anonymized data: player IDs and positions, league format settings, model inputs and scores, and the reason and the outcome.
  - Never store league IDs, team names, owner names, usernames, SWIDs or cookies.
  - Say so plainly in the UI and in the privacy policy, give users an opt-out toggle, and rate-limit submissions.
- **Built at Checkpoint 7a** (planner reply to Checkpoint 4c, B2; `src/lib/feedbackShare.ts`, `feedbackEvent.ts`, `src/server/feedbackSink.ts`, `src/app/api/feedback`):
  - **When:** a card's outcome or Report press is saved in the browser first, as before; once kept, and while Settings' **"Share anonymous feedback"** is on (the default; per browser, localStorage `offersheet:feedback-share:v1`), the record as it now stands goes as one anonymous copy to `POST /api/feedback`, 2 s later, debounced per record (Sent then Accepted sends one copy), once, with keepalive, failures silent; a waiting copy goes at once when the page hides. Turning sharing off, or undoing the press before the copy leaves, sends nothing. Presses on one card are shared in the order they were made, each copy built from the record as that press alone left it. A press that removes a record sends a take-back copy (no outcome or reason). The first copy a browser sends shows once, in the card's status line: "Sent anonymously to help improve Offer Sheet. You can turn this off in Settings."
  - **What a copy holds** (`FeedbackEventSchema` in `src/server/feedbackSink.ts`, a strict zod schema on the server: unknown fields refused at every level, every string a closed set or an exact pattern, every number bounded): a random share id per local record (`rid`, 16 random bytes, derived from nothing; later copies of the record carry it, so the latest copy per id is the record), kind (outcome or report), the outcome or reason, the version sent and, when it isn't the card's trade, that version's players (`versionTrade`), the platform, the season, week and goal the numbers were computed for (the record's `basis`; an older version's "balanced" kept as recorded: `FEEDBACK_GOALS`), with (Checkpoint 7c; planner reply to Checkpoint 7b, §6: "the anonymous copy holds only the shares, rounded to whole percents, never the amounts") under "Several prizes" the prize split those numbers were weighed by (`prizes`: each prize's whole percent of the season's prizes, adding up to 100, `reward.prizePercents`, e.g. Parker's 44 / 36 / 20 / 0 / 0; the same in the sent version's inputs; taken from the engine's reward, `TradeSearchResult.reward` / `TradeAnalysis.reward`, from the same source as the goal: both trade pages build a copy's context through `feedbackShare.ts` shownRewardOf from the result they show, never from the settings' goal, which under "Several prizes" would read as an unknown goal, and `feedbackShare.test.ts` checks both pages call it (review FB-R3); never the amounts typed, which stay in the browser's settings), the league format (teams, league type, scoring keys, lineup slot counts by the positions a slot takes, playoff teams), the trade's players (canonical ids in the exact forms the app makes: Sleeper ids, team-defense codes, `espn:<id>`; and positions; no partner id), the card's numbers (Helps you, chance as printed, Overall, B, B₃, P_accept, P_complete, ΔPO, ΔCH, ΔPPW, both sides' g), the acceptance features and the sent version's inputs (with B₃, the scale's reason and its week). **Never:** league ids, team ids, team or owner names, usernames, SWIDs, cookies, the league's short hash, typed notes, IP addresses or a time finer than the day. A trade with a draft pick, or ids of another form, sends nothing.
    - The prize split's rules (Checkpoint 7c; `feedbackSink.ts` PrizesSchema, `feedbackStore.ts` isFeedbackPrizes and recordReward, `feedbackShare.ts` feedbackRewardOf): whole percents from 0 to 100 on exactly the five prizes, adding up to exactly 100, so amounts ($2,000 as 2000) or finer shares (44.4) never fit; a split only with goal "prizes", and that goal only with one (at the top and in the version's inputs; a refusal is logged as INVALID_PRIZES or INVALID_VERSIONINPUTS); optional in the schema, so an older version's copies (which never name "prizes") and the ones stored before still read. ⚠️ Ours: "Several prizes" whose split can't be read (a goal alone, a record from a build before the split was kept) reads as an unknown goal (null), in the record and the copy, so every copy the browser builds passes; "Several prizes" with nothing entered (1st place weighed as everything, `Reward.empty`) sends 100 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0, what its numbers were weighed by; the version's split is its own when the card gives one, else its press's, like its week (tradeView.ts writes the goal alone). No ΔE[S] in the copy (the lead's call, for the size bound). The bound's test now builds its biggest copies at the browser's own precision (`sig`, 6 significant digits: at most 14 characters a number within the schema's bounds), with the split at both levels: the biggest copy the browser can build is about 1,950 bytes as stored, under 2,048 (at full precision, which no copy holds, the test's copy was 2,096). `POLICY_FEATURES.prizeShares` is on since then: the privacy page says the copy holds "how your league splits its prizes (as shares, never amounts)" (`pages.test.ts` ties the switch to any prize field in the schema, both ways).
  - **The store:** Upstash on Vercel only; each copy plus its UTC day (nothing else about the request) goes to one list per UTC day (`os:<env>:fb:YYYY-MM-DD`; final review SEC-3: a flood can only crowd out its own day) in one transaction (LPUSH, LTRIM to the newest `feedbackLimits.maxPerDay` 100, PEXPIRE to `retainDays` 365 after the list's UTC day began, so a day's list goes a year after that day began and no copy outlives the privacy page's "up to 12 months" (Phase 7b, `feedbackDayTtlMs`, the error log's rule; before, each write reset it to a full year, so a day's first copy could stay up to a day longer); 3 commands a copy); a stored copy is at most 2,048 bytes. Each visitor may send 30 copies a day and its group 60 (counted under the same HMACs as the work budget, deleted a minute after the UTC day ends); past that 429. No store (development, the stable copy, tests): 202 `{ stored: false }`; a failed write is logged by name and answers the same. A refused copy is logged as `feedback_invalid` with the failing field's code only, at most once every 10 minutes per instance. Rate limit: 60 copies an hour per IP (in each instance's own window since Phase 7b: Section 3). About 1 KB a copy; at most about 75 MB at the caps (45 MB with real copies).
  - **Reading it** (Phase 7b; planner reply to Checkpoint 7a, §4 privacy item 3: "read straight from Upstash and save only aggregate results and fitted numbers locally, never raw records"): `npm run feedback:report -- --live` (`--env production`, `--days N`) reads the day lists straight from the store (one LRANGE a day), checks every entry against the store's schema (one that doesn't parse is counted, never printed or kept) and keeps only totals, on the console and in git-ignored `data-local/feedback-report.md`: copies, records (a record's copies share its `rid`; the latest is the record) and take-backs; per record its outcome, reason, version sent, goal (an older version's "balanced" counted as its own), platform and league type; (Checkpoint 7c) of the records under "Several prizes", how many pay each prize (1st, 2nd, 3rd, the weekly high score, the most points: its share above 0), counts only, never a record's split; the acceptance calibration (each record's predicted P_accept in the label bins against Accepted and Rejected); the refit's readiness; and the days to check (a day at the store's cap or far above a usual day: Phase 6 drops or down-weights them). Never a copy, an id, a player, a record's own numbers or a note. Phase 6's refit reads the store the same way and keeps only the fitted coefficients. It replaced `npm run feedback:pull` (Checkpoint 7a), which saved the copies themselves to `data-local/feedback-server/` and came with the privacy page's promise that the developer runs it at least monthly; that promise is gone ("We keep anonymous feedback for up to 12 months, then delete it."), and feedback:report says when an old `data-local/feedback-server/` folder is there, to be deleted. The local record keeps its `rid`; since Checkpoint 7d the export doesn't carry it (it holds what the user saw and entered: above), so an exported file is matched to its server copies only by content: its trades, the days it holds and the Helps you, chance and Overall its cards printed, against each copy's players, UTC day and printed numbers (`helps`, `chance`, `overall`; the forms map, review of Checkpoint 7d, cards-export-5: a chance of "<5%" is a copy's `chance` 0, "—" its null; a Helps you or Overall of "0" is a copy's 0, or its null when its `b` is at most 0, a trade that didn't help; "—" is its null otherwise), as Section 14 says (the privacy page's "Email us to ask what we hold about you …").
- **How the data is used:**
  - "They'd never accept" and Rejected outcomes refit the acceptance coefficients. Once there are at least 30 outcomes, a Node script refits the β coefficients with logistic regression, using an L2 prior that pulls toward the defaults.
  - "Doesn't help me" triggers a review of the benefit and projection logic for those cases.
  - "Player info is wrong" and "League settings look wrong" go to a bug queue for the adapters.

**Phase 6 sanity check**
- Pull recent completed trades from public Sleeper leagues. Find leagues by snowballing through league member lists, and stay under the rate limits.
- Check that at least 80% of those trades have |g| ≤ 0.20 at current values.
- Report the distribution.

---

## 12. Trade search

**User settings**
- max players per side: default 2, allow 3 (Settings offers 1 to 3)
- "Protect my trade value": Low / Medium (default) / High, redraft leagues (Section 10; 1 / 2 / 4 points a week per 100% of trade value since Phase 7b)
- untouchables
- must-include players
- positions to target or avoid
- include P, K, DEF and IDP (excluded by default, Section 9)
- "What does your league reward?": Only the champion (default) / Several prizes / Making the playoffs (Phase 7b's words, planner reply to Checkpoint 7a, 5.2; Phase 4c's "What matters most": Win the title / Balanced / Make the playoffs; since Checkpoint 7c "Several prizes" in place of "Top finishers too", planner reply to Checkpoint 7b, §6, with the league's prizes: 1st, 2nd and 3rd place, the weekly high score and the most points in the regular season, `TradeSettings.prizes`, prefilled 60 / 30 / 10; a league saved with "Top finishers too" reads as "Several prizes" on that split; Section 10). It weighs B and sets the automatic playoff-week weight (Section 7), so changing it, or the prizes under "Several prizes", searches again (the engine worker's context is keyed by both: `LEAGUE_MODEL_SETTINGS`).
- no playoff-week weight setting since Phase 7b: the weight is automatic (Section 7), and Settings shows it read-only
- "Chance they accept: at least" Any / 10% (default) / 20% / 30% / 40% / 50%, the Trade Finder's filter (Phase 4c, Section 13)
- include draft picks (dynasty leagues). ⚠️ Phase 4: deferred to Phase 5 (a planner question at Checkpoint 4): the model inputs carry no pick values yet (FantasyCalc's pick rows are kept apart and not passed on) and only Sleeper and MFL report traded picks. The setting is kept; with it on in a dynasty or keeper league the search says "Draft picks aren't part of trade ideas yet.", and the Analyzer offers players only.

**Steps**

1. **Precompute** p, LV, ROS, MV_add and MV_lose for every team. Memoize LV by roster hash and week.

2. **For each partner P:**
   - **Give pool G:** my top 10 players by surplus score, plus every trade chip of mine (Section 7), excluding untouchables.
     - Surplus score = z(v_i) − z(MV_lose(me, i)), with both z-scores taken within my roster.
     - Sort highest first: high market value, small loss to my lineup.
   - **Get pool H:** P's top 10 players by `MV_add(me, j)`, keeping only those above 0. Since the planner's addendum (§2): the top 10 by MV_add(me, j) among untagged players, plus the top 3 "Harder to get" players, so star trades are still evaluated and rank on their honest acceptance.
   - **Must-include players** always go into the right pool, and every candidate trade must contain them.
   - **Screening objective** = my ΔROS (fast lineup method) × quick P_accept.
     - The quick P_accept is the Section 11 model using g, n (fast lineup method), h, c, a, hist and ctx.
     - It leaves out hot, urg and the veto rule.
   - Enumerate every 1-for-1 in G × H and score each one with the screening objective.
   - Take the best 60 and extend each by one more player, either from G (my side) or from H (their side). That produces 2-for-1, 1-for-2 and 2-for-2 trades.
   - Phase 4c (planner reply to Checkpoint 4b, change 1): also every 1-for-2 and 2-for-1 from the whole give and get pools, and every 2-for-2 from the first 6 of each pool (`search.exhaustive.twoForTwoTop`), through the same screening and funnel. Beam extension missed two-player packages when neither half helped alone (Wilson → Chuba Hubbard + Mike Evans).
   - If the max per side is 3, extend the best 40 of those once more, the same way.
   - Drop any trade whose g_mkt(P) falls outside [−0.35, 0.6].
   - Keep this partner's top 30 by the screening objective.

3. **Exact evaluation.** Take at most 200 trades across all partners, the best by screening objective. For each: check legality, work out drops and adds, solve exact lineups for both teams over the availability scenarios (Section 7), then compute n, h and P_accept. Since Checkpoint 7d (planner reply to Checkpoint 7c, answer 2) also up to 40 more, the helps-both lane: screened trades the shortlist doesn't hold on which both lineups gain ("Checkpoint 7d details" below).

4. **Screening simulation.** Run 1,000 simulations with common random numbers on every exactly-evaluated trade to get a first B.

5. **Final simulation.** Take the top 60 (40 before Phase 4c) by B × P_complete and rerun them with 5,000 simulations. This gives the final B, Trade Score and P_complete. Since Checkpoint 7c (planner reply to Checkpoint 7b, §7) also the top 20 by Helps you among the trades that help both on the screening run (since Checkpoint 7d one more for each of those from the helps-both lane: "Checkpoint 7d details" below), checked again on the final run ("Checkpoint 7c details" below), and since Checkpoint 7d the finalists the lane's trades push out, for the "Helps both" list only (⚠️ ours: "Checkpoint 7d details" below). Since Checkpoint 7e (planner reply to Checkpoint 7d, item 1.1) these 5,000 seasons are the ranking block, the first 5,000 of the shown block's 20,000 (Section 8, "Common random numbers"): the B, Trade Score and P_complete they give are the ones the search ranks and chooses on, and the cards it shows get the shown pass (step 6), which gives the B, Trade Score and odds the cards print (P_complete doesn't depend on the seasons).

6. **Rank and diversify** (Section 13) and return the top 25; since Checkpoint 7c also the second list, "Helps both", from the same finalists and the step 5 extras (which only that list ranks). Since Checkpoint 7e, **the shown pass** ("Checkpoint 7e details" below): every card of either list and every Plan B box is simulated again on the shown block's 20,000 seasons, every number a card prints comes from that run, and the rules the lists and boxes are chosen by hold on those numbers.

**Performance target**
- 10 seconds or less for a 12-team league on a mid-range laptop, in the Web Worker, with a progress bar.
- Report actual timings at each checkpoint.

**Phase 4 details** (`src/engine/evaluate.ts`, `search.ts`; lead decisions M1–M8 and the reviews)
- **The context**, built once per search or Analyzer session: the League model's steps (projections, scenarios, values, streaming, every team's V*(T) and lineup costs, chips, surplus scores) with no simulation of its own; the baseline is every team at V*(T), each team's weekly score built from V*(T)'s lineups as TeamModel.score is (streaming rows included). ⚠️ A week in progress keeps each team's League-model week-0 score (rosters as they stand, before kickoff) before and after every trade: a trade or pickup can't change a week under way; a trade made before kickoff counts the current week in full (the platform's review period isn't modeled). Partner profiles, hot players, urgency, values, hole medians, slot ranks and my risky weeks are computed once. Notes that stop the search (the Analyzer still evaluates, except without a team): no team picked, no weeks left, my team certainly out in a redraft league, the deadline passed, no odds.
- **Pools:** active-roster players with a model (Phase 4b: and IR players with a model; taxi players never). G = my top 10 by surplus score (an IR returner's lineup cost is his removal cost from V*(me)'s lineups) after the filters (untouchables, excluded positions), plus my chips and my must-include players (must-include always stays, even when untouchable). H = each partner's top 10 untagged and top 3 "Harder to get" players by MV_add(me, j) in EV lineups (League-model rule) above 0, after the position filters (planner addendum §2), plus their must-include players. A partner's IR player is priced as a returner on V*(me) when I'd have an open IR slot and he's eligible, else like any player. Must-include ids on no active roster or IR are ignored with a note; on two or more other teams: no trades, a note; one partner's: only that partner is searched.
- **Screening:** EV lineups from V*(me) and V*(P) minus what goes plus what comes, legality in EV mode (since Phase 7c the spot a trade opens takes the best free agent at a position given up by EV gain, Section 7's same-position pickups), ⚠️ not re-optimized (for speed); objective = my ΔROS × quick P_accept; exact shortcuts re-solve only the weeks a trade can change (checked against fresh solves on 2,800 trades). An extension adds one player from G, one from H, or one to each side (pairing the 5 best single additions per side); max per side 2 extends the best 60 seeds once (2-for-1, 1-for-2, 2-for-2), 3 extends the best 40 of those once more. Then the g_mkt(P) window [−0.35, 0.6] (market values floored at W, without the star premium), 30 per partner, 200 overall. Ties everywhere: the objective, then the key.
- **Exact evaluation:** legality in scenario lineups (its keep values, and since Phase 7c the spot a trade opens, filled at a position given up by scenario gain: Section 7's same-position pickups, where a given player the side's own baseline cuts anyway opens no spot, as in screening; until then the fill was judged by EV gain), then both sides' V* from scratch on the post-legality rosters (since Phase 7c never dropping a legality add: the optimizer's keep set), scenario lineups, n, h, g, the card's numbers and the explanation facts. Since Phase 4b the two sides go in free-agent allocation order, each from its own pool (Section 7, "One free agent, one team").
- **Simulations:** the baseline's setup with only my team's and the partner's rows replaced (common random numbers). The screening (1,000 seasons) and the final run (5,000) use separate blocks of one matrix, so the final numbers come from seasons that played no part in choosing the trades (review of track E: reusing them biased the shown B upward, +0.26 on average); until Checkpoint 7e the Analyzer and the baseline odds used the final block. Title odds are exact per season (Section 8). Since Checkpoint 7e three blocks of one matrix (Section 8, "Common random numbers"; `evaluate.ts` SimState): the shown block (20,000 seasons: the baseline odds, the League page's headline, every profile, λ and note read; the cards and boxes shown; the Analyzer), the ranking block (the "final" stage, its first 5,000: the finalists, extras and Plan B candidates), the screening block (the next 1,000). Each block keeps its own baseline, "Helps you" calibration and partners' "Helps them" runs (`SimState.rank` for the ranking block's), and every stage is scored on one block's scale: the screening and ranking stages on the ranking block's (so the search ranks as it did on 5,000 seasons), the shown stage on the shown block's (`scaleBlockOf`). ⚠️ The shown block holds the ranking block's seasons, so a card's shown numbers still read the 5,000 that ranked it, a quarter of its 20,000 (the lead's design). With `shownSims` at most `finalSims` the blocks are one and the search is Checkpoint 7d's bit for bit (on Parker's week-6 dump, and `reward.pinned.test.ts` runs so).
- **Dominance pruning** (⚠️ lead decision N11, reported at Checkpoint 4; in the spirit of §13's filler rule): after the beams and the g window, a screened trade that gives one of my players worth at most the waiver credit W (less than W before Phase 4c) is dropped when the same trade without him (my side keeping a player) is inside the value window too and screened at least as well. Under §9's waiver credit (and Phase 4b's value floor, which counted him at W) such a player pulled the partner's g toward 0 and added a player to the trade; since Phase 4c's neutral throw-ins he moves neither g and only adds a player (the complexity term), so these variants are dominated, yet they took 85 of the 200 exact-evaluation slots on Parker's league (the 2026-10-01 data; 77 on the live data of 2026-10-03, 9 with the pruning).
- **Finalists:** the 60 (40 before Phase 4c) picked with §13's per-core, per-partner and (Phase 4b) per-given-player caps one looser (my must-include players exempt since Phase 4c), their window covering every finalist (Phase 4b final review: a top-20 window let finalists 21–40 of the then 40 be mostly players already at a cap, and the list showed 16 cards), then filled by EG, never one hidden by default; since Phase 4c they are chosen without the minimum chance, so a new minimum re-filters them without a new search. The strict §13 rules and the minimum chance apply in the final ranking (with Phase 4's 40 finalists, 21 of the 25 a full 5,000-season ranking of all 200 would show made it). Then the ranking (rank.ts), then the offer-first steps for the shown trades (§13) and their words (explain.ts, after the steps, so the pitch names the offer-first version).
- **Determinism and progress:** the same inputs give the same result; the generator yields every 100 screens, every 5 screening simulations and after each exact evaluation or final simulation (typically under 40 ms of work between yields on Parker's league in Node; a garbage-collection pause or a cold start can exceed it, and the report measures the longest; ⚠️ since Checkpoint 7e a run on the shown block is a unit of its own, with a yield before and after it, of about 45–50 ms on Parker's 10-team league and 60–65 ms on his 12-team one in Node: 20,000 seasons can't be split), so the worker can slice and stop it. Parker's league (final numbers, the 2026-10-01 data, Node, median of 3): context 0.26 s, screening 2.0 s, exact 1.8 s, screening simulation 0.5 s, final simulation 0.5 s, steps and words 0.1 s, about 5 s in all; in the production browser worker (live league, 375 px) a first Trade Finder visit took 5.1–6.8 s and a warm search 2.8 s. Phase 4b (the 2026-10-04 data, Node, warm median of 3): context 0.25 s, screening 1.7 s, exact 1.25 s, screening simulation 0.5 s, final simulation 0.47 s, steps, Plan B and words 0.19 s, about 4.1 s; the same search again on its context 2.9 s; in the production browser worker 4.3 s (`npm run check:browser`).
- **Consistency (Checkpoint 4):** before and after both use V* judged in the lineups B is measured in, so no trade gets credit for a pickup I could make anyway: a test reproduces a sub-threshold depth pickup with a trade and gets ΔPO = ΔCH = ΔEW = 0 exactly, in EV and in scenario lineups.
- ⚠️ Known approximations: screening isn't re-optimized; screening fills a spot a trade opens by EV gain, exact evaluation by scenario gain, so the two can pick different players (Phase 7c); a spot is filled greedily, one at a time, and a position is short by primary position only (Section 7); a free agent a trading team gives up goes to nobody when a team sits between the two in the allocation order (Section 7); IR players: a player received from an active roster never uses an open IR slot, teams never move their own active OUT players into one, and the displacement is applied to final evaluations only (Section 7); the review period isn't modeled (Phase 6); picks are deferred (Phase 5).

**Phase 4c details** (planner reply to Checkpoint 4b; `src/engine/search.ts`, `rank.ts`, `pattern.ts`)
- **Exhaustive candidates** per partner on top of the beams (step 2): deduplicated by key with the beams, then the same legality, objective, g window, dominance pruning (since 4c a player worth at most W, as the neutral rule), keep-30 and 200 cap. ⚠️ Must-include players are in every candidate and don't count among a 2-for-2's first 6. With max 3 per side the third player extends the best 40 two-player trades among beams and exhaustive alike. `TradeSearchOptions.exhaustive: false` (a report switch) keeps the beams alone.
- **Lean companions** (⚠️ ours, in the spirit of change 2: a throw-in worth less than a free agent can only lower the chance they accept through value, so the app shouldn't suggest a pointless extra player): every trade that reaches exact evaluation and contains players the neutral rule leaves out brings its lean version (the same trade without them, when both sides keep a player, every must-include player stays and its g is inside the screening window) into exact evaluation too, on top of the 200, one per trade; the funnel decides between them, and the filler rule drops the bigger variant when the lean one ranks above it. Screening isn't re-optimized, so legality's free-agent add in the opened roster spot could make the bigger variant screen better (on the 2026-10-04 00:51 UTC data, live order, before the companions, Doubs + Nix → Metcalf was shown at 43.1% while Nix → Metcalf, never evaluated, reached 47.2% with the same Helps you). Companions skip the N11 pruning; stand-ins (cheap players left in for a roster-spot credit) bring none. `TradeSearchOptions.leanCompanions: false` (a report switch) turns them off.
- ⚠️ A cap on how many exact-evaluation slots may give the same player of mine (to fill the list) was measured and left out (the 2026-10-04 00:51 UTC data, live order: 17 of 25 cards without one): at most 3 trades per player within each partner's keep reached 21 cards alone, and 24 only together with at most 20 or 30 over all 200, both times by displacing a top-10 card (Harrison Jr. + Nix → Metcalf, Overall 6) for a lower one (Sutton → Goedert, Overall 5). On that data the 200 exact trades give a helping trade to only 8 of Parker's players, so the given-player cap binds first.
- **60 finalists** (`search.finalists`), chosen without the minimum-chance filter, so re-filtering is instant and consistent; no promise of a full list: on Parker's data 14 of 25 cards at 10%, because the 200 exact trades give only a few of his players and the given-player cap binds first.
- **The minimum chance** (Section 13) is applied in the ranking; `refilterSearch` re-ranks the same finalists for a new minimum and rebuilds the shown list's steps, Plan B, words and pattern note, reusing every evaluation already made (it equals a fresh search with that minimum, tested); the worker runs it without a new search.
- **Block order:** the final simulation reads seasons 1–5,000, the League model's own, and the screening the next 1,000 (Section 8), so a card's "before" is the League page's headline exactly.
- Parker's league (the 2026-10-04 09:24 UTC data, weekly reset order, Node, Checkpoint 4c): 17,215 trades screened (the beams alone 13,603), 206 evaluated exactly (the 200 and their lean companions), 60 finalists, 14 cards; warm median of 3: context 0.24 s, screening 2.45 s, exact 1.46 s, screening simulation 0.53 s, final simulation 0.71 s, steps, Plan B and words 0.23 s, 5.6 s in all (cold 5.7 s); a re-filter takes 11–38 ms; in the production browser worker a full search took 5.1 s (`npm run check:browser`, live data). Wilson → Hubbard + Evans is screened #1 of Team 4's 1,783 candidates and shows as card #3 (Helps you 30 / 35% / Overall 12); the beams alone never screen it.

**Checkpoint 7c details: "Helps both"** (planner reply to Checkpoint 7b, §7; `src/engine/search.ts` finalistsOf, finish; `rank.ts` RankOptions `order` and `keep`; `TradeSearchResult.helpsBoth`; Helps them and "helps both": Section 10)
- **The partners' scales first:** before the screening simulation, each partner of the exact trades gets its calibration run (`evaluate.partnerCalibrationOf`), one at a time with a progress yield after each, so no slice holds several (about 13 ms each on Parker's league).
- **The extras:** on top of the 60 finalists, the first `config.search.helpsBothFinalists` (20) exact trades by B on the screening block (ties: EG, then the key) that help both and aren't hidden by default (⚠️ the lead's call: the plain top 20, no caps in the choice; since Checkpoint 7d one more for each from the helps-both lane, below) get the final simulation too; those that weren't finalists already (`SearchState.helpsBothExtras`, since Checkpoint 7d with the finalists the lane displaced, `SearchState.laneDisplaced`, below) count in counts.simFinal. ⚠️ Only the second list ranks them (review of this track, finding HB-5: they're simulated so that list has enough candidates, and on the Overall list a trade that missed the finalists got a second chance only because it helps the partner; on 42 synthetic leagues extras changed which cards the Overall list showed in 4, one card pushed out under the core cap, and its counts in 24 more), and Plan B's pool keeps their screening numbers, as before them.
- **The second list** (`TradeSearchResult.helpsBoth`): every finalist, the extras too, ranked by `rankTrades` in Helps-you order (`order: "helps"`, B highest first, the order §13's caps go through them in), keeping only the trades that help both on their final numbers (`keep: helpsBoth`, the planner's re-check: an extra that no longer helps both stays off), after the §10 guards and before the minimum chance, with §13's caps (one partner and must-include players as the Overall list's; ⚠️ the filler rule judging each pair as the Overall order does, review of this track, finding HB-3: in Helps-you order a bigger variant whose extra player lifts B a little came first however much less likely, ahead of its smaller trade, which is no superset of it; on Parker's league under his prizes the list led with Puka Nacua + Marvin Harrison Jr. → Drake London + Christian Watson, B 1.454 at 24.7%, then Puka Nacua → Drake London + Christian Watson, B 1.440 at 27.2%. So `rank.ts` reads the kept cards in Overall order after the pass, a card that is filler of an earlier card that stays leaves, and the list is ranked again without it, the freed place going to the next trade; ⚠️ when the trades that take the freed places push its smaller trade off the list after all (a cap it needs), the card that left comes back and its smaller trades leave instead, for good (final review of Checkpoint 7c, engine-rank-hb3-late-filler: on made-up leagues, with 2 players a side too, neither showed; `rank.ts` fillerRounds, judged once no filler is left. A scratch check of 200,000 random lists: 35 left out a card no rule explains before, none after, and Overall order the same in all; Parker's searches unchanged); in Overall order nothing ever leaves that way, so that list ranks exactly as before), its own folding of cards that show one plan, `qualifying`, `byChance` (its trades under the minimum) and `lowerWouldAdd` (the same finalists at Any would show more: the page's suggestion of a lower setting; never at Any). `rank.ts` sortTrades "helpsBoth" (the trades that help both first, by B) and helpsBothTrades (a list filtered and sorted) serve a page without the engine's list.
- **One evaluation per card:** steps, Plan B and words are made once per card for both lists (the Overall list's cards first); a card on both lists is the same object, which the worker's structured clone keeps one. ⚠️ A card that helps both teams (its tag, on its final numbers, on either list) offers first only a version that helps both, else it has no steps (`evaluate.ts` tradeSteps, `StepOptions.helpsBoth`; review of this track, finding HB-1: on Parker's league under his prizes the card Garrett Wilson + Bo Nix → Nico Collins, Helps them 14, said "Offer first: Garrett Wilson for Nico Collins", Helps them 0, and its message proposed that), and its Plan B box helps both too, on the box's final numbers (`planB.ts` planBPasses; finding HB-4: both Team 8 cards there offered Garrett Wilson → Christian Watson + Carnell Tate, Helps them 0, and on 42 synthetic leagues 21 of the list's 27 boxes didn't help both; a box shows no "Helps them" line). Judged by the card, never by the list it's on, so a card's steps are one (both lists fold on them) and the tag the page shows in every sort holds for what the card suggests; the Analyzer's steps follow the same rule. ⚠️ A Plan B box names no card of its card's list, nor a version that list's steps show, nor a trade that a card of that list with that partner matches or beats (barredFromBoxes and dominatedByCard); a card on both lists reads both lists' cards (its one box is read in either). Finding HB-2: reading both lists for every card took boxes from Overall cards for trades only the second list shows, though the page shows one list at a time. ⚠️ The second list's boxes keep to the given-player cap among themselves in its own top-20 window, the boxes of its cards on the Overall list counted first. So the Overall list (cards, steps, boxes, words, `few`, the pattern note, every count but the final runs) is what it is without the second list (tested: the same search with every partner's scale unknown, so that no trade helps both), but for a card that helps both, whose rules can also move the cards after it (its folding, the box cap: 2 of 42 synthetic leagues). The pattern note stays the Overall list's. Progress counts one unit per card of either list. Re-filtering rebuilds both lists and equals a fresh search (tested).
- Parker's league (Checkpoint 7c's numbers: its bar, Helps you above 0, and no helps-both lane; "Checkpoint 7d details" below give his numbers on the same data under his prizes at Checkpoint 7d's bar, without the lane and with it; the 2026-10-04 09:24 UTC data under his prizes, $2,000 / $1,600 / $900, Node): of 201 exact trades, 171 help him on the screening run, 30 help the partner (B_P > 0), 8 read Helps them ≥ 10 and 5 help both; of the 60 finalists 3 help both on their final numbers; 2 extras, 1 still helping both; none of the 12 Overall cards (Helps them: eleven 0, one 4). The list shows 2 cards, none on the Overall list, each one offer with no box (no trade with its team passes Plan B's rules and helps both): Team 8, Puka Nacua → Drake London + Christian Watson (Helps you 30, Helps them 38, 25%; their title odds 16.7% → 19.2%, top 3 50% → 54%, playoffs 85% → 88%) and Team 6, Garrett Wilson + Bo Nix → Nico Collins (2 / 14 / 40%; 13.8% → 13.8%, 47% → 48%, 89% → 89%; an extra). Before the review's fixes it showed 3: Puka Nacua + Marvin Harrison Jr. → Drake London + Christian Watson (31 / 38 / 25%) ahead of its smaller trade (HB-3), both Team 8 cards with a box that didn't help Team 8 (HB-4), and the Nico Collins card with "Offer first: Garrett Wilson for Nico Collins" (HB-1). The Overall list is exactly the one before "Helps both" (Checkpoint 7c's wave 4), its steps, boxes and words too, under his prizes and under "Only the champion". Under "Only the champion": 5 of 203 exact trades, none of the 60 finalists, 5 extras all still helping both, 3 cards (Puka Nacua → Chris Olave + Christian Watson, 50 / 13 / 10%; Puka Nacua → Drake London + Christian Watson, 28 / 42 / 25%; Kyle Monangai + Dalton Schultz → Jadarian Price, 12 / 11 / 30%, one offer: its lean version Kyle Monangai → Jadarian Price doesn't help Team 6). The extra runtime, about 0.15 s on a 5.7 s search, cold or warm: the 8 calibration runs (one per partner of the exact trades) about 0.10 s, the extras' final runs 0.03 s, the second list's steps, Plan B and words 0.01–0.02 s. ⚠️ A question for the planner: the list can only promote what exact evaluation already holds, the 200 trades chosen by my gain × the quick chance, and few of those help both (answered at Checkpoint 7d: the helps-both lane, below).

**Checkpoint 7d details: the helps-both lane** (planner reply to Checkpoint 7c, answer 2: "take them from every screened trade, not just each partner's kept 30. Add up to 40 to the exact shortlist where both lineups gain on the fast method: your ΔROS > 0 and their lineup gain > 0 … best screening objective first, skipping trades already on the shortlist … After that: they go through the same exact evaluation, screening simulation and finalist rules as every other trade"; `src/engine/search.ts` helpsBothLaneOf, `config.search.helpsBothLane`)
- **Which trades:** every partner's screened trades after screening's rules (legal, holding the must-include players, inside the g window and past the dominance pruning: ⚠️ the lead's reading, these are screening's rules and the keep isn't one), on which both lineups gain on the fast method: my ΔROS (`Screened.myDelta`, the screening objective's own number) and the partner's ΔLineup_P (`Screened.partnerDelta`, the number the quick acceptance's n is made from: ⚠️ the lead's reading of "their lineup gain"), both above 0 (float noise aside), that the shortlist and its lean companions don't hold; the first 40 by the screening objective, then the key.
- **Then the same rules:** evaluated exactly after the shortlist and its companions, each bringing its lean companion like a shortlisted trade (⚠️ not counted in the 40), and from then on exact trades like any other: the screening simulation, the finalists' rules (so a lane trade can be a finalist and a card on the Overall list) and the "Helps both" extras. `SearchState.lane` keeps the lane's keys, its companions' and how many screened trades qualified. `TradeSearchOptions.helpsBothLane: false` (a report switch) or the config at 0 leaves it out; a search whose qualifying trades are all on the shortlist already is the same with it or without (tested). Deterministic, and a re-filter equals a fresh search (tested).
- Parker's league under his prizes ($2,000 / $1,600 / $900; `trade:report`, Node): on the 2026-10-04 09:24 UTC data 161 screened trades gain both lineups, 56 of them on the shortlist or among its companions; the lane takes 40 and 4 companions; the exact trades that help both on the screening run go from 3 of 201 to 8 of 245, the extras from 0 to 5 (2 still helping both on the final run), and the "Helps both" list from 1 card to 3: Team 8, Puka Nacua + Bo Nix → Chris Olave + Christian Watson (Helps you 46, Helps them 11, 10%; from the lane), Puka Nacua → Drake London + Christian Watson (30 / 38 / 25%) and Team 6, Rico Dowdle + Bo Nix → Jadarian Price (11 / 15 / 35%; from the lane). On the week 5 data (2026-10-09 18:00 UTC): 223 qualify, 32 on the shortlist; 40 and 1 companion; 13 of 200 → 25 of 241; extras 1 → 9 (8 still helping both); finalists the ranking picked that help both 11 → 15; the list stays at 5 cards, 3 of them from the lane (Team 8: Breece Hall → Christian Watson + Carnell Tate, 39 / 16 / 10%; Brock Bowers → Chris Olave + Juwan Johnson, 28 / 41 / 20%; Puka Nacua + KC Concepcion → Drake London + Christian Watson, 26 / 40 / 35%). ⚠️ The list goes by Helps you with at most 3 cards a partner in its top 20, so there the lane's trades, more helpful and less likely, took Team 8's places from likelier ones (Breece Hall + KC Concepcion → Christian Watson + Dallas Goedert, 22 / 12 / 40%; Breece Hall → Christian Watson + Dallas Goedert, 20 / 12 / 45%; Puka Nacua + Kyle Monangai → Drake London + Christian Watson, 15 / 41 / 50%).

As first built (the plain top 20) the lane's long shots could push a likelier one out, and a trade without a final run can't reach the list: on `smallLeague` 13 at 10% seven lane trades, each under 5% as printed, entered the 20 and the 15th (on the list at 10 / 33 / 10%) fell to 24th, so the list went from 2 cards to 1; of 60 synthetic leagues (`smallLeague` 1–60 at 10%, 8 scenarios) the list was shorter in that 1, longer in 14 and the same length in 45. With the wider room (measured by the lane's reviewer through `helpsBothFinalists`) the list is never shorter than without the lane and longer than the plain 20 gave in 4 of those leagues, for 61 more final runs in all (up to 14 in one league), the Overall list the same in all 60; on Parker's four searches the same lists, with 2 more final runs on week 5 under his prizes. The lane's trades also take finalists' places like any other trade (the next bullet), and the pool reads the screening run, so a finalist they pushed out that helps both only on its final numbers lost its final run, and with it its place on the list (the final review, ENG-7D-1: on `smallLeague` 45 at 10% two lane trades took finalists' places, and one of the two finalists they pushed out read Helps them 0 on the screening run and 14 on the final one; at 20% and 30%, where the lane's long shots are under the minimum, the list without the lane shows it at 84%, the list with it didn't). So (⚠️ ours) each finalist the ranking would pick without the lane's trades and their companions that it doesn't pick with them keeps its final run when its Helps them is known (a trade whose partner's scale is unknown can't help both): `SearchState.laneDisplaced`, among `helpsBothExtras`, ranked on the "Helps both" list only, with its screening numbers in Plan B's pool, so the Overall list is what it was. Of 60 synthetic leagues (the same searches) the lane displaces 168 finalists in 44, one of them helping both on its final numbers (`smallLeague` 45's); on Parker's four searches 3 and 5 (2026-10-04 data, his prizes and "Only the champion") and 10 and 7 (week 5), none helping both, so the same lists, for about 12 ms a final run (warm, week 5 under his prizes): +36 to +121 ms a search (⚠️ derived). Tested: every final run without the lane whose Helps them is known is made with it, on 8 synthetic leagues (`smallLeague` 45 among them, its list at 20% showing every card the list without the lane shows); `trade:report` section 10 counts any that isn't (`laneShown`'s `finalsLost`) and the finalists kept (`laneCounts`). Not chosen: picking the extras only among trades that print at least the lowest minimum above Any (long shots would then never reach the list, even at Any).

On the 2026-10-04 data under "Only the champion" (3 of 14 cards changed) two lane trades giving Bo Nix took 2 of his 4 places in the finalists' ranking, so Bo Nix → Jadarian Price (15 / 35% / Overall 6) missed the finalists and Rico Dowdle + Bo Nix → Jadarian Price (16 / 30% / 5) took card #10; under his prizes 1 of 12 changed (2026-10-04) and 2 of 12 (week 5). "Checked N closely" goes up by 41–45 (under his prizes 201 → 245 and 200 → 241; under "Only the champion" 203 → 248 and 201 → 242) and "Left out on purpose: N trade ideas that wouldn't help you" by the lane's trades whose screening B isn't above 0, 9–13 of its 41–45 (29 → 39, 28 → 37; 24 → 37, 31 → 40). On 60 synthetic leagues (`smallLeague` 1–60 at 10%, 8 scenarios) the Overall cards change in 26, rank by rank on EG better in 22, worse in 1 and mixed in 3; the boxes of cards both show change in 11 (22 cards), the steps in none; the left-out count rises in 53 and never falls. So the Overall changes are mostly gains by the list's own measure. Kept, as the planner asked ("the same … finalist rules"; the lead's call): ranking lane trades on the "Helps both" list only, as the extras are (finding HB-5 of Checkpoint 7c), would set them apart from every other trade and give up those gains along with the worse #10; the "Helps both" list loses no final run to them either way (the bullet above).

**Checkpoint 7e details: the shown pass** (planner reply to Checkpoint 7d, item 1.1: "If any trade's Helps you moves by more than 3 points across seeds: raise the final simulation for the cards shown to 10,000 or 20,000 seasons, and report the runtime"; the lead's design, as built; `src/engine/search.ts` finish, "The shown pass"; `evaluate.ts` the three blocks; `config.simulation.shownSims` 20,000)
- **Ranked as before, but for the screening block's seasons:** the screening simulation (step 4) and the offer-first steps run on the screening block, now seasons 20,001–21,000 (after the shown block; 5,001–6,000 before), so the exact trades' screening B, the finalists, the hidden counts and the "Helps both" extras can differ from Checkpoint 7d's by draw; the final simulation (step 5) and the ranking run on the ranking block (5,000 seasons, the shown block's first: Checkpoint 7d's final block's seasons): the finalists' order, both lists' membership and folding, Plan B's candidates and choice, `few` and `lowerWouldAdd`, each scored on that block's B₃ and partners' scales. ω, now measured on the 20,000 seasons (Section 7), moves the pickups behind every number.
- **Then every card of either list and every Plan B box** is simulated once more on the shown block (20,000 seasons) against its baseline (the League page's headline), on its B₃ and each partner's "Helps them" scale there (made when first needed, one calibration run a yield), once per key (`SearchState.shownByKey`): a card the first time a list holds it, before its steps are made; a box when its card's choice reaches it. Every number a card or a box prints comes from that run (B, Helps you, Overall, the odds lines, the weekly line, Helps them, the "Helps both" tag), and its words are made on it. The Analyzer evaluates on the shown block too, so a card opened there reads the same numbers, steps and message.
- ⚠️ **The rules hold on the printed numbers** (ours, the review of the track: SHOWN-1 to SHOWN-3), as before Checkpoint 7e, when a card printed the numbers it was chosen on:
  - a card's "Helps both" rules for its steps and its box (Checkpoint 7c's HB-1 and HB-4) read its printed tag, so its steps and message are the Analyzer's (before the fix, `smallLeague` 127's card read Helps them 10 on the ranking block and 8 as printed, and showed neither steps nor a box where the Analyzer offered its lean version first);
  - each list is `rank.ts`'s ranking of its own cards on their printed numbers, in its own order (Overall by EG, "Helps both" by B): nothing comes in, but a card whose shown B isn't above 0 leaves (§10's guard: counted among the trades that wouldn't help you), one whose printed numbers don't help both teams leaves the "Helps both" list (the lead's call), and one §13's rules drop in the printed order leaves, counted among the variety rules' drops (before the fix the re-sort moved a card from past the 20th place into the top 20 in 3 of 40 twelve-team synthetic lists at Any, and gave a partner or a player of mine a 4th card there in 2). None is refilled;
  - a box is chosen on the ranking numbers in its list's printed order (the box cap's top-20 window too), then held to Plan B's rules on its printed numbers against its card's and its list's (half the card's Helps you, helping both under a card tagged "Helps both", no card of its list matching or beating it), else the next candidate is tried within `planB.tries` (before the fix 19 of about 1,180 boxes on 300 synthetic searches printed numbers that broke one, 17 of them under half their card's Helps you).
  On `smallLeague` 1–150 at 10% under each goal and 40 twelve-team searches at Any, no box, list or cap breaks a rule on the printed numbers after the fix; against the same searches without the pass the Overall lists lose 2, 3 and 3 cards, the "Helps both" lists 9, 10 and 7, and of 574, 593 and 401 boxes 5, 13 and 11 go with none in their place (1, 2 and 3 come, 1, 1 and 6 change). ⚠️ Not done: a card whose printed numbers help both and ranking numbers don't is tagged "Helps both" (its steps and box held to the rule) but doesn't join the "Helps both" list, whose cards the ranking chose (1 card in the 150 searches under "Only the champion", 2 under "Several prizes"), as a card that list's caps leave out doesn't. The steps stay on the screening block and the ranking block's scale, each with its own B₃ (`TradeStep.b3`, which the feedback's version inputs record; the review's SHOWN-4; item 4.1 of the planner's reply replaces the steps). `few` and `lowerWouldAdd` compare the rankings at Any and at the minimum. The pattern note is made on the list shown.
- **Progress:** under the "rank" stage, so a re-filter still reads as one: its units each card's numbers and steps, each run on the shown block and each Plan B, planned up front so the bar only moves forward (the review's SHOWN-5: the pass restarted it, and the bar sat at 96–99% for about 1.2 s of Node time on Parker's league, now about 0.2 s; 1.25 s and 0.26 s on his 12-team one), with `TradeSearchProgress.part` "shown", which the Trade Finder names "Playing out the ideas you'll see 20,000 times"; the stage holds the bar's last 16% (4% before: `tradeView.ts` STAGE_WEIGHT). Its own timing key, "shown": the runs on the shown block.
- **Re-filtering** ranks again on the ranking block and simulates on the shown block only the trades no earlier list held (a re-filter equals a fresh search, tested). `TradeSearchOptions.shownPass: false` (a report and test switch the app never sets) prints the ranking numbers, as before; with `shownSims` at most `finalSims` the pass reads the ranking numbers as they are: either way Checkpoint 7d's search bit for bit.

The review's fixes change nothing printed here (the same cards, steps, boxes and counts with them and without). Across the 5 seeds the top 5's Helps you spread by 1 / 1 / 2 / 1 / 2 (Section 8).
