(Q3; Section 10; `SettingsView.tradeValueNote`). "Export feedback" leaves out the value ratios behind the estimates (Q5; the export's version 2, Section 11; ⚠️ their fields: the estimates it kept could still give them back). Since Checkpoint 7d (planner reply to Checkpoint 7c, answer 1; version 3, Section 11) the file holds only what the user saw and entered, and the feedback card says so (`FeedbackCard.tsx` `EXPORT_TEXT`): "The file holds what you saw and entered: each trade (its players and the other team as ID numbers), the numbers on its card, your outcome, the version you sent, the problem you reported and your notes, with the day you first marked or reported each trade and the days of its latest outcome and report. Apart from what your notes say, it has no names, and it numbers your leagues (league-1, league-2) rather than saying which they are."
   - Checkpoint 7c (planner reply to Checkpoint 7b, §6, league prizes; `settingsView.ts` SettingsView.prizes, parsePrizeText, prizeCommit, prizeSplitText; `SettingsForm.tsx` PrizesGroup): the goal's answers read "Only the champion" (default) / "Several prizes" / "Making the playoffs" ("Top finishers too" went; a league saved with it reads as "Several prizes" on the prefilled split: Section 10), "Several prizes" with "Weighs your share of the league's prizes most (85%), playoff odds 15%.", and the group's help, the same for every answer (the lead's words): "Helps you scores a trade by how much it raises your chances at what your league pays and of making the playoffs. Pick what your league's prizes reward." (dynasty and keeper: "For this season, …(how much later seasons count is set under Win now or build for the future). …"), the bubble line kept after it. Under "Several prizes" only, **"Your league's prizes"**, a fieldset of its own after the goal's (a sibling, so the goal's group keeps its three labeled answers): the planner's helper line verbatim as its description ("Enter each prize in dollars or percent, the same way for all. Only the split matters. Leave blank what your league doesn't pay."), five labeled 44 px text fields with a decimal keyboard ("1st place", "2nd place", "3rd place", "High score, each regular-season week", "Most points in the regular season"; prefilled 60 / 30 / 10, the rest blank), then the split as the engine reads it, whole percents adding up to 100 ("Your prizes split: 1st 44%, 2nd 36%, 3rd 20%." for Parker's 2,000 / 1,600 / 900; the weekly high score's for the whole season with how many times it's paid, "weekly high scores 10% (paid 14 times)", over the league's prize periods; "<1%" for a paid prize that rounds to none), or, while the amounts add up to nothing, "Enter at least one prize. Until then, Helps you counts the title only." A field keeps what's typed as a draft and saves it on blur or Enter, never per keystroke (each saved change rebuilds the League model for the playoff weight), read leniently ("$2,000", "60%", "1 600", "1,000,000"; blank, nothing but spaces, for not paid; a comma only between groups of three digits); text it reads as the saved amount saves nothing; text it can't read isn't saved and says why under it, aria-invalid with the message linked by aria-describedby and in a polite live region that stays mounted, so it's announced though it shows on blur, after focus has moved on (review of the prizes UI, prizes-ui-3): "Enter a number, like 2000 or 60." (a lone "$" or "%" too, which had saved as blank and erased the prize: prizes-ui-4), "A prize can't be negative.", "Enter a smaller number.", and "Use a period for decimals, like 12.5." for a decimal comma ("12,5", "5,00": some phones' decimal keypads type it; it had read as 125 or 500: prizes-ui-4); Escape drops the draft, and a saved amount that changes under it (Reset to defaults) replaces it. The amounts are kept whatever the answer. The playoff weight's fallback for unknown odds reads "your prize odds aren't known" when it was measured under "Several prizes" (`fallbackWords`; the title's words otherwise).

**Home page and privacy**
- A placeholder landing page for Offer Sheet is already live (Section 17, Phase 7). The app's home page should carry over its wording: what the app does, how it works, and the platforms it supports.
- The privacy policy lives at `/privacy`. It must match what the app actually does, so update it whenever data handling changes.
- Built at Checkpoint 7a (planner reply to Checkpoint 4c, B3; `src/lib/site.ts` holds the landing page's words, the theme colors, `CONTACT_EMAIL` and `siteUrl()`):
  - **Home:** the live landing page's title ("Offer Sheet — fantasy football trade finder"), description and Open Graph words, the app's three numbers, a platform list with status (ESPN and Sleeper available; Yahoo "Waiting for Yahoo's approval"; Fleaflicker, MFL, Fantrax and manual entry "Later"), and a "Beta" pill next to the logo (a word, not color alone; tighter below 640 px so the league switcher keeps the logo's row down to 320 px).
  - **/privacy, /terms, /feedback:** the text lives in `PrivacyPolicy.tsx`, `TermsOfUse.tsx` and `FeedbackGuide.tsx`; each sentence about a Part B feature shows only while its switch in `POLICY_FEATURES` is on (browserReads, anonymousFeedback; Phase 7b added fantasyCalcHidden, publicArchive and feedbackTotalsOnly, on since 2026-10-05; Checkpoint 7c, planner reply to Checkpoint 7b, §3, added dailyLevels, on: the archive sentence names the daily rest-of-season levels the cron saves under `snap:lvl:`, "its daily rest-of-season projections in standard scoring formats"; prizeShares, on since the anonymous copy's server schema holds §6's prize shares (`src/server/feedbackSink.ts` PrizesSchema; the test ties the switch to any prize field, under any name and at any depth, each taking whole percents, never amounts): "how your league splits its prizes (as shares, never amounts)" in place of "how you weigh the title against the playoffs"; and helpsThem, on since §7's "Helps both" sort and the "Helps them" score on the cards and the Analyzer's (the test ties it to any sort label with "Helps both" and the words anywhere the app's pages are worded): "Helps them" among the terms' estimates; all eight on, the terms' "Last updated" kept at launch day since the switch went on before it), and `pages.test.ts` fails when a switch disagrees with the code, both ways (each of Checkpoint 7c's three is also turned on alone and must change only its own words; the change that builds §6 or §7 turns its switch on). Numbers (retention, windows, caps) are read from `src/server/limits.ts` and config. Whoever changes the text sets `PRIVACY_LAST_UPDATED` / `TERMS_LAST_UPDATED` (October 13, 2026 since 2026-10-07: the launch day, Parker's choice; changes before launch keep it, as Checkpoint 7c's did, and a switch turned on after launch moves its page's date) and regenerates `reports/privacy-terms-draft.md` (`npx tsx data-local/policy/policy-to-text.ts --write`; the planner can't open the app). /feedback explains the card buttons and the anonymous copies and has no form (nothing the public types is stored on the server). Checkpoint 7d (planner reply to Checkpoint 7c; no switch: both rest on this checkpoint's own code): the terms' data line names DynastyProcess's license, "player ID maps from DynastyProcess (shared under the GPL-3.0 license)" ("Also"; the footer links its repository, "Footer and layout" below), and the feedback page's export sentence says what the file holds (answer 1), in both branches after their own lead-in: "“Export feedback”, also on the Settings page, downloads what you saw and entered: each trade, the numbers on its card, your outcome, the version you sent, the problem you reported and your notes. It also holds the day you first marked or reported each trade and the days of its latest outcome and report." (without sharing: "“Export feedback” on the Settings page downloads what you saw and entered: …"; ⚠️ the lead's words but for the problem reported and the days, added at the review of Checkpoint 7d, PD7D-1: the file holds them, and the Settings card already said the days); `pages.test.ts` ties those words to the export itself (a made-up record's model numbers, share id and trade key never reach the file, its note does, and every field a record holds has its words on the page). ⚠️ If Checkpoint 7c launches and 7d follows after launch, the terms' date moves to the day 7d deploys. Contact (Phase 7b; planner reply to Checkpoint 7a, 3): "Contact: offersheet.app@gmail.com" (`CONTACT_EMAIL`; until then "Contact: to be added before launch"), and no page, metadata, manifest, footer or sharing image names a person, only "Offer Sheet" (a test checks). Just before the privacy page's contact line, and only there: "Email us to ask what we hold about you or to have it deleted." (Checkpoint 7c; planner reply to Checkpoint 7b, §3; ⚠️ we can find only what a person points us to: mail from their address, and their anonymous copies by what a feedback file they export holds, since Checkpoint 7d the trades, the days and the card's numbers, matched to the copies' (the file no longer carries the random code that links them: planner reply to Checkpoint 7c, answer 1; the privacy page's "Only your browser, and a file you export, can tell which copies are yours" holds that way); no tool deletes a copy yet, so that's by hand in Upstash's console).
  - **The shorter policy** (Phase 7b; planner reply to Checkpoint 7a, §4; the final text: `reports/privacy-terms-draft.md`): "The short version" stays first, with "Offer Sheet sets no cookies, except one encrypted cookie if you connect a private ESPN league." and "per-visitor limits, counted under codes made from your IP address with a secret key, deleted within a day"; the implementation detail users don't need is gone (the browser database's name, tabs' session codes and messages, the old storage keys and their migration, how trade-page addresses carry ids, the in-memory reuse of signed imports, the limits' windows and counts). New facts: "We keep anonymous feedback for up to 12 months, then delete it." and "When we study it, we keep only totals and the numbers our estimates learn from it, never individual copies." (the monthly-download promise is gone); the public data kept to measure accuracy (Sleeper's pre-game projections and FantasyCalc's daily lists, up to 2 seasons, about no league or person beyond which formats were in use each day, never shown); the daily usage totals (about 13 months: the counters' 400 days); Upstash in US East with backups off; FantasyCalc's values used in our own numbers but never shown, a format's list not asked for again for 3 hours once the server has it, and only when needed, and its links telling it only that a visit came from Offer Sheet; the Gmail inbox. ⚠️ The detailed part is held at 1,300 words by a test since Checkpoint 7c (planner reply to Checkpoint 7b, §3: "raise the test's cap to 1,300 words to fit these"; 1,273 as shown and with every switch on; 1,250 and 1,249 at Checkpoint 7b; 3,042 at Checkpoint 7a): the planner asked for about 1,000, and the next cuts would drop facts. The iPhone storage sentence says "may" (one secondary source). Terms: "Your part" in the planner's words (connect only leagues you're in or may view, don't use Offer Sheet to collect information about other people, keep your ESPN cookies private), "You must be at least 13 to use Offer Sheet.", and trade values no longer among the numbers Offer Sheet quotes.
  - **Installable:** `manifest.ts` (id "/", start_url "/league", display standalone, theme #0b1f33, icons 192 and 512 "any" plus a separate 512 "maskable"), `icon.svg`, an opaque 180 px `apple-icon.png` and a PNG-in-ICO `favicon.ico`, all drawn by `scripts/make-icons.ts` (sharp, offline) from one mark (`src/components/logoGeometry.ts`: the app's navy football on a gold tile). No service worker.
  - **Sharing and search:** metadata with a title template and `metadataBase` (https:// + `VERCEL_PROJECT_PRODUCTION_URL`, else http://127.0.0.1:3000; never a canonical or og:url in the root layout), `opengraph-image.tsx` (1200×630, static), `robots.ts` (disallow /api/), `sitemap.ts` (the 5 public pages), `noindex` on /import, /league, /trades, /analyzer and /settings, and branded not-found, error and global-error pages that show nothing of an error's message.
  - **Headers** (`next.config.ts`): production builds send a full CSP (`default-src 'self'`, inline scripts and styles allowed for Next's bootstrap, `connect-src 'self' https://api.sleeper.app https://lm-api-reads.fantasy.espn.com`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`; the Vercel Toolbar's sources only on preview builds; `OFFERSHEET_CSP_REPORT_ONLY=1` at build sends it report-only); `next dev` keeps frame-ancestors only. Plus X-Frame-Options DENY, nosniff, Referrer-Policy same-origin, a Permissions-Policy that leaves the clipboard allowed, and no X-Powered-By.
  - **Errors from the host** (`errorForStatus`): a 502 or 503 that isn't our JSON (Vercel's own page; a paused Hobby project answers 503 for up to 30 days) reads "Offer Sheet isn't available right now. Try again later."; a 504 "Offer Sheet took too long to answer. Try again in a few minutes."; a firewall's 429 states its wait.
  - **Import Report:** a "League data read by" row ("Your browser, straight from ESPN" / "Offer Sheet's server") and Refresh at most once a minute. **Settings:** the "Share anonymous feedback" switch on the "Your trade feedback" card (Section 11).

**Footer and layout**
- The footer shows:
  - "Trade values: FantasyCalc" with a link

  - Checkpoint 7a: a links row "Feedback · Privacy · Terms" (44 px tap targets), the FantasyCalc link, the credits ("Projections and stats: Sleeper and ESPN · Player IDs: DynastyProcess · NFL schedule: ESPN"), "Offer Sheet is a free, independent beta. It is not affiliated with or endorsed by ESPN, Sleeper, Yahoo, Fleaflicker, MyFantasyLeague, Fantrax, FantasyCalc, DynastyProcess or the NFL." and "© 2026 Offer Sheet".
  - Phase 7b: "Trade values: FantasyCalc" is a plain link in the root layout's server-rendered footer, so it's in every page's HTML, with FantasyCalc's attributes (rel "noopener", referrerpolicy "origin"; Section 2.9). The credits name exactly the sources production uses (nothing from nflverse, which was Phase 0's cross-check only).
  - Checkpoint 7d (planner reply to Checkpoint 7c, "Also": "Link 'DynastyProcess' in the footer to its data repository"; `reports/security-review.md`, finding 10): "DynastyProcess" in the credits links to https://github.com/dynastyprocess/data, where the map and its GPL-3.0 license are (`SiteFooter.tsx` `DYNASTYPROCESS_LINK`: a plain link in the same server-rendered footer, a new tab, rel "noopener" like FantasyCalc's links (⚠️ ours: not the "noopener noreferrer" of the privacy page's links to other services' policies, since noreferrer adds nothing here, the site's Referrer-Policy, same-origin, sending another site no referrer; review of Checkpoint 7d, PD7D-6), and no referrerpolicy of its own; inline in its sentence, so not a 44 px target); the credits' words are as before. Both of the footer's links to other sites, and the privacy page's three to other services' policies, open a new tab and say so to screen readers (review of Checkpoint 7d, PD7D-2): each one's aria-label is its words and " (opens in a new tab)" (`SiteFooter.tsx` `NEW_TAB`), what ValuesCredit's link reads out in a screen-reader-only span, so the words it shows stay its own (check:browser's DynastyProcess step wants exactly "DynastyProcess", and the privacy page's word count reads only what it shows).
- Design mobile-first and make it work at 375 px wide. Keep it clean, sporty and easy to read.
- Phase 4 finish: every button and link meant to be tapped is at least 44 px tall on phones, the Import Report's and the Connect page's small buttons included.

---

## 15. Config defaults (`src/engine/config.ts`)

This is the single source of truth. Every number listed here must be read from config, never hardcoded.

The server's numbers for the public beta (Checkpoint 7a: cache tiers and timeouts, rate limits, budgets, deadlines, upload caps, the token's age, the error log and the feedback store) live in `src/server/limits.ts` instead, one block per part; Section 3 and `reports/beta-infrastructure.md` give the values. Phase 7b added `healthLimits.checkEveryMs` (a store check at most every 5 minutes per instance), `snapshotLimits` (the cron: lock 10 minutes, 12 FantasyCalc formats a UTC day, kept 730 days, the formats in use kept 3 days, 5 s per store command, a format started only with 15 s left, values at most 1,000,000 characters; Checkpoint 7c added `levelsMinTimeLeftMs`, the day's levels started only with 45 s left), `usageLimits` (counters kept 400 days, held refusals written at most every 5 minutes, FantasyCalc's 10,000 sessions a month with a warning from 8,000), `modelBuildLimits` (8 League page builds at once per instance, a waiting build gives up with less than 30 s left, Retry-After 60), the log throttle (`logLimits.refusalEveryMs` 10 minutes, `repeatEveryMs` 1 minute), `rateLimits.health` 12 and `cron` 10 per 10 minutes and `sharedKinds` (import, model), `deadlines.cronMs` 110 s, and in `budgets` FantasyCalc's day of 1,500 (200 before), `reservedPerClientPerDay` (ESPN 50) and `countAheadMinMs` (1 s), and `workLimits.unpackedUnitBytes` (500,000).

| Key | Default | Section |
|---|---|---|
| actualWeight / actualFullGames; actualWeightByPos | 0.25 / 6; K 0, DEF 0 (Checkpoint 7e: a kicker's or team defense's base rate is his projection alone; every other position 0.25) | 6 |
| weekShapeClamp | [0.8, 1.2] | 6 |
| injury table | see Section 6 | 6 |
| sigma c/d by position | see Section 6; P uses K's (an assumption) | 6 |
| NA availability | 0 every week | 6 |
| future injury risk: M_pos (long-run share of games missed) | QB 0.06, RB 0.14, WR 0.12, TE 0.14, K 0.02, DEF 0, IDP 0.10 | 6 |
| mean injury length d | 2.5 weeks | 6 |

---

## 16. Testing

- **Vitest unit tests** for every engine function, plus:
  - property tests of the lineup solver against brute force
  - the scoring oracle tests (Section 5), covering every rule type
  - the acceptance anchor tests (Section 11)
  - ESPN private-league error paths (401, 403, HTML login page), tested with mocks until a real private league is available
- **Simulation sanity tests:**
  - Σ P(title) = 1
  - Σ P(playoffs) = playoffTeams
  - a no-op trade has exactly zero delta under common random numbers
- **Adapter tests** run on sanitized fixtures, with no network.
- `npm run probe:all -- --live` runs the live checks by hand (every probe needs `--live` since Checkpoint 7a, Section 0.9).
- The checkpoint reports, `npm run league:report` and `npm run trade:report`: offline on the newest saved dump of Parker's league by default (`--dump <folder>`, `--team <id>`, `--out <file>`, `--live`: Section 0.9; `scripts/lib/runMode.ts` reportArgs). Checkpoint 7c (planner reply to Checkpoint 7b, §6): `--prizes <1st>,<2nd>,<3rd>,<weekly>,<leader>` gives the league's prizes as the Settings page takes them under "Several prizes" (plain numbers, dollars or percent, one unit for all, the weekly prize one week's; blank or 0 not paid, at least one above 0; `prizesArg`; in PowerShell a list with a blank inside goes in quotes), and the header's command repeats it. Every comma starts the next prize, so amounts typed with thousands separators are refused only when a group starts with 0 ("2,000,1,600"), and "2,500,1,500" reads as four prizes, 2 / 500 / 1 / 500. So both reports say how they read the flag before the long run (review FB-R2): the amounts at once (`runMode.ts` prizesReadLine, which adds how to type thousands when an amount of 100 to 999 follows a whole number under 1,000; ⚠️ ours: a hint, never a refusal, since 500 / 300 / 200 is a fine split), then, once the league is loaded, the Settings page's own split line for them (`league-report.ts` prizesSplitLine: "Your prizes split: 1st <1%, 2nd 6%, 3rd <1%, weekly high scores 83% (paid 14 times), most points 11%." for `--prizes 2,500,1,500,900` in Parker's league); the reports' own words name every paid prize the same way ("<1%" for one that rounds to none). With it every "Several prizes" figure reads the split (without it ⚠️ the prefilled 60 / 30 / 10), and a split that pays the weekly high score is never priced from runs that didn't simulate them (reward.ts assertWeeklyMeasured). Most come from a League model or trade context built under the split as the app builds them (its own ω and pickups): league:report's "League prizes" section (how 3rd place is decided; under "Only the champion" and under the split, each from its own model: ω, B₃, P(1st), P(2nd), P(3rd), the top 3, P(playoffs)), the app's own B₃ under each goal and the playoff-week weight's table; trade:report's section 2, the app's search under the split on its own context against "Only the champion" (both top 5s and top 10s, each side's chance), and section 3's table of each goal's B₃ and ω. The scale check's "Several prizes" column doesn't (league:report's "Helps you" scale check; trade:report section 3's scale check and its table's "read" column): it prices the split on the default goal's baseline (that goal's ω and pickups), as it does every goal's weights, so the goals compare on one run (review FB-R4; Parker's B₃ there 3.92 ± 0.12, 3.98 on the split's own model). The reports name the goals as Settings does ("Only the champion", "Several prizes", "Making the playoffs"). Parker's (`--prizes 2000,1600,900`: the top 3 only, 44 / 36 / 20%, on the 2026-10-04 09:24 UTC dump): ω 1.06× (1.98× under "Only the champion"), B₃ 3.98 (4.94), P(1st) 10.6%, P(2nd) 8.3%, P(3rd) 11.2%, top 3 30.1% (10.5%, 8.3%, 11.2%, 30.1%); 3rd place by a game between the two semifinal losers (assumed in that dump, which predates the adapter's read; Phase 0's raw capture of his league has `consolationLadderDisabled` false, which the adapter reads as a game, ⚠️ its reading); 4 of his top 5 trade ideas the same.
- trade:report's section 10, "Helps both" (Checkpoint 7c; planner reply to Checkpoint 7b, §7): under "Only the champion" and the `--prizes` split, how many trades help both at each stage, read off each search's state (`helpsBothCounts`: the exact trades on the screening run, the extras, the finalists the ranking picked, the Overall cards, the "Helps both" list), the split's list (its first 5 cards: Helps you, Helps them, the chance they accept, the partner's title, top-N and playoff odds before → after) with each partner's "Helps them" scale under the split (`evaluate.partnerScaleOf`: B_ref,P, which is B₃,P unless it mixes in long-term value, then printed as both), and the extra runtime (`helpsBothCost`), measured under "Only the champion": each of section 4's runs reads its partner calibration runs and its extras' final runs off its own progress (`helpsBothParts`), and each timed one is followed by the same search without "Helps them" (`withoutHelpsThem`: every partner's calibration memo set to none, the engine test's way; its Overall list the same), whose ranking stage gives the second list's work; the extra is the three parts, and the total without it section 4's less them (⚠️ derived: the runs without it are measured too and said against section 4's with their own sign, with each set's run-to-run spread of the search totals and of setup, screening and exact evaluation alone, which Helps them doesn't touch, from 2 timed runs a set: `measuredRunsText`; review of the report track, HBR-1: the sentence had the sign wrong and named a cause it never computed). Section 4 counts final runs, the finalists and the extras apart (`finalRunsText`), and sections 8 and 9 read the ranking's finalists (`rankedFinals`). Parker's at Checkpoint 7c (its bar, Helps you above 0, and no helps-both lane; Section 12's "Checkpoint 7d details" give his numbers on the same dump under the split at Checkpoint 7d's bar, without the lane and with it; `--prizes 2000,1600,900`, the 2026-10-04 09:24 UTC dump): under the split 5 of 201 exact trades help both (8 score Helps them 10+), 2 of them extras (1 still helps both on the final run), 3 of the 60 finalists, none of the 12 Overall cards, a list of 2 (Team 8: Puka Nacua → Drake London + Christian Watson, Helps you 30, Helps them 38, 25%; Team 6: Garrett Wilson + Bo Nix → Nico Collins, 2, 14, 40%); under "Only the champion" 5 of 203, all 5 extras, a list of 3; B₃,P under the split 1.24 (Team 2) to 3.85, none floored; the extra runtime, measured under "Only the champion" (section 4's runs, 5 extras): about 0.2 s on a 5.8 s search (warm: 8 partner calibration runs 102 ms, 5 extras' final runs 63 ms, the second list 36 ms; cold 5.89 s, about the same extra; the runs without it measured 91 ms under section 4's, and a set's search totals spread over up to 246 ms from run to run).
- Checkpoint 7d (planner reply to Checkpoint 7c, answer 2; Section 12): section 10 also counts the helps-both lane under each setting (`laneCounts`: the screened trades on which both lineups gain, those the shortlist and its companions held, the lane, its lean companions and the legal ones; of those, the ones that help both on the screening run, the finalists the ranking picked, the extras, those that help both on the final run and the cards of each list; and the finalists its trades displace, kept for the "Helps both" list, `SearchState.laneDisplaced`). The extras' row counts the room as the engine does (`search.helpsBothPoolOf`: 20, one more for each candidate from the lane; the final review, ENG-7D-2: it had counted the plain 20, and printed a negative "already finalists" past 20 candidates), the same stage counts as above for the same search without the lane (`TradeSearchOptions.helpsBothLane: false`: under "Only the champion" section 4's runs, each timed one now also followed by one without the lane, `timedSearches`' pairedNoLane; under the split section 2's search against one on a context of its own), how many Overall cards the search without it doesn't show (`cardsNotIn`), what else it changes on the Finder (`laneShown`; review of the lane, LANE-1 and LANE-2: the Plan B boxes and offer-first steps of the cards both show, the "Helps both" list's cards each way, the final runs of the search without it whose Helps them is known that it doesn't make, a guard that reads 0 (`finalsLost`; the final review, ENG-7D-1), and the "checked N closely" and "Left out on purpose" counts, without → with), and what the lane costs (`laneCost`): its exact evaluations and screening simulations read off each run's own progress (`laneParts`: the search evaluates and simulates the lane's trades last, ⚠️ a batch of 5 simulations' time shared out evenly where the lane's begin inside one), the rest (the final simulation, ranking and steps) with the lane less without, warm only, and the runs without it as measured. Section 4's exact count says how many came from the lane (`exactText`: "248 exact (45 from the helps-both lane: section 10)"), its re-screening rebuilds the lane and checks it is the search's (`SearchGap.reproduces.lane`), and the named trade's funnel says when it came through the lane. Section 10's words and its first row follow answer 5 (a trade helps both when Helps you and Helps them, as printed, are each at least 10: the row counts Helps you 10+, above 0 before). The report's title reads Checkpoint 7d. Parker's numbers: Section 12, "Checkpoint 7d details".
- `npm run check:browser -- --live --team <id>` (Phase 4b; after `npm run build`; `--live` since Checkpoint 7a, Section 0.9) runs the production build in headless Chrome on Parker's league at 375 px: every page, a full Trade Finder search with its timings, a card's buttons, the Analyzer and Settings; no sideways overflow, no console errors, tap targets of at least 44 px, "Harder to get" never on his own players. The league id is read from `.env.local` and never printed; output and screenshots are cleaned of league names and stay in git-ignored `.cache/check-browser/`. Phase 4c: it reads saved data from IndexedDB (never creating the database), and `--second-league` adds the public ESPN test league 899513 through "Add league", checks every page shows it with its own settings and feedback, switches back (a partner picked on one league's Finder doesn't follow), and removes it from a trade page whose address names a team.
- Checkpoint 7a: check:browser also fails on any Content-Security-Policy violation (enforced or report-only, in a page or the Web Worker: workers report them only as CDP Audits issues), checks the Public pages (Home, Privacy, Terms, Feedback, a missing page, then the site files: headers, manifest and icons, the sharing image, robots.txt and the sitemap) even after a failed import, checks the header's first row at 320 px with the real league switcher, reports which path read the league ("League data read by"), and counts only the league's own data requests to ESPN and Sleeper as platform reads (any other request there, or to another host, fails).
- Phase 7b: check:browser also covers Checkpoint 7b (`scripts/check-browser/contracts.ts` holds the rules, `steps.ts` the steps): no FantasyCalc value or value comparison on the cards, the League page (every player breakdown opened) or the Analyzer; FantasyCalc's links (rel exactly "noopener", referrerpolicy "origin") in every app page's server HTML; no code, path or stat id above the Import Report's technical details, and a Notices step (the "Things to know" card, banner and warnings box speak plainly); the Waivers step and the nav's two rows of three at 375 px; the Trade Finder's freshness and best-time lines; Settings' goal wording, read-only playoff weight and trade-value help; the Analyzer's back link not prefetched. An "info" line (a new status, not a failure) says where the league's own data differs from what a check expected of it (with `--team`: Parker's league showing a notice or banner the planner expected it not to, its scoring card's quiet line reading differently, the blend reaching nobody).
- Checkpoint 7c: check:browser covers the league prizes (`steps.ts` settingsPrizeChecks and prizeOddsChecks; `contracts.ts` prizeGroupProblems, invalidPrizeProblems, oddsLabelProblems, headlineTopProblems): Settings' "Your league's prizes" group under "Several prizes" (the planner's five prizes and helper line, 44 px fields, the split line; Enter saves; text it can't read explained in a live region and not saved); in a fresh browser the League, Trade Finder and Analyzer steps see no top-N line; then, with "Several prizes" saved from the Settings step, the League headline's "N% top 3", the Finder's cards and Plan B boxes and the Analyzer's card read "Top 3 odds" (review of the prizes UI, prizes-ui-1), before the old answer is put back. And §7's "Helps both" (`steps.ts` cardStep, cardOddsChecks, analyzerStep; `contracts.ts` cardHelpsBoth, helpsBothTagProblems, helpsBothListProblems, helpsBothStatusOk, oddsLabelProblems with `whose` "theirs"; `inPage.ts` cardData's helpsThem, theirOdds and helpsBothTag): the card step's four sorts (Helps you, Chance they accept, Helps both, then Overall, so the page is left in its default order) never search again; in every sort the "Helps both" tag is on exactly the cards that help both teams as printed (Helps you above 0 at Checkpoint 7c, at least `config.benefit.helpsThemMin` since 7d; Helps them at least `config.benefit.helpsThemMin`); the "Helps both" sort shows only those, by Helps you (`sortInversions` "helpsBoth"), its status line saying how many ("Found 2 trade ideas that help both teams.", "No trades help both teams right now."); every card's partner's line names their odds as mine are named ("Their title odds", "Their top 3 odds" under "Several prizes", "Their playoff odds"); the Analyzer's card shows the Finder's card's "Helps them" and tag (Checkpoint 7d: below). `FINDER_DONE` reads every status line the Finder's done state can show (`FINDER_DONE_STATUS`, tested on every sort and empty state), so a Finder remembered in another sort still reads as done.
- Checkpoint 7d (planner reply to Checkpoint 7c, answers 3 and 5, and "Also"; `contracts.ts` cardHelpsBoth, finderPartnerLineProblems, analyzerPartnerLineProblems, linksInHtml, dynastyProcessLinksInHtml, dynastyProcessLinkIssues): the "Helps both" tag needs Helps you at least `config.benefit.helpsThemMin` too; on the Trade Finder, in every sort, a card shows the partner's line ("Helps them N" and their odds) exactly when it carries the tag, and an "If they say no, try:" box never does; the Analyzer's card always shows it ("Helps them 0" when the trade costs them), with the same tag as the Finder's card for the same trade, the Finder's number when that card showed the line, and the tag its own printed scores call for (review of Checkpoint 7d, cards-export-3: a Finder card shows Helps them only with the tag, so only the Analyzer's card can show a trade that helps both but is tagged nowhere); and a step of its own, "DynastyProcess link": every app page's server HTML has the footer's "DynastyProcess" link to exactly https://github.com/dynastyprocess/data, rel exactly "noopener", shown without JavaScript (from the same HTML as FantasyCalc's step).
- Phase 7b's guards in the unit tests: `src/lib/fantasyCalcHidden.test.ts` (no FantasyCalc value or comparison on the cards, the Trade Finder, the Analyzer with its Details, the League page with every breakdown, Settings or the Import Report, built from fixtures with distinctive values; Settings is also read for an exchange rate between trade value and points; since Checkpoint 7c also the "Helps both" sort with its helper line, the tag and the partner's line on the Finder's cards and the Analyzer's, a dynasty card's "Their long-term value", and How to read's Helps them paragraph in every league type, with long-term values and without; since Checkpoint 7d also the feedback export, a file users open: what the cards printed, every press's, and a record made before 7d printed from its own numbers), `src/lib/notices.test.ts` (every warning code has its rule), `src/server/clientBundle.test.ts` (no client entry, a "use client" module or a Web Worker, reaches server code or a server-only package), `src/server/nextConfig.test.ts` (the image optimizer stays off and nothing imports next/image), and `src/app/pages.test.ts` (the policy pages' words both ways, the privacy page's detailed part at most 1,300 words since Checkpoint 7c, 1,250 before, no person's name; since Checkpoint 7c also each of the three new switches tied to its code, dailyLevels to the cron's `snap:lvl:` save, prizeShares to any prize field in the anonymous copy's server schema (under any name and at any depth, each holding whole-percent shares, never amounts), helpsThem to a "Helps both" sort or "Helps them" anywhere the app's pages are worded, and turned on alone to change only its own words; since Checkpoint 7d also the terms' DynastyProcess line, and the feedback page's export words in both branches, tied to the export itself: a made-up record's model numbers, share id and trade keys never reach the file, its note does, and the words name every field a record in the file holds, the league aside, which the file only numbers: review of Checkpoint 7d, PD7D-1).
- Checkpoint 7a's offline measurements: `npm run measure:cache` (the shared cache: reducers proved equal on every cached body, sizes, Upstash commands and bytes per session through FakeRedis, CPU) and `npm run measure:upload` (browser uploads: sizes, server CPU, identical to the server's own read). `npm run beta:log -- --live`, and since Phase 7b `usage:report`, `feedback:report` (it replaced `feedback:pull`) and `snapshot:pull` (each with `-- --live`), read the shared store (read-only since 2026-10-07: `READONLY_STORE_URL` and `READONLY_STORE_TOKEN` typed into .env.local by hand, Upstash's read-only token under names the app never reads, no SESSION_SECRET; `scripts/lib/storeReader.ts` gives them the three read commands they use, and before the first read deletes a key nothing writes, which fails closed (review of the follow-ups, SEC-1): the reader opens only when Upstash refuses that DEL with its permission error (NOPERM), on the first try or on a second one a second later when the first got no clear answer; a carried-out DEL (a token that can write), or no clear answer twice, stops the script, and nothing is read; ⚠️ Upstash's wording of the refusal is assumed until the first live run: were it worded otherwise, every run would stop with "Couldn't confirm …", never reading with a token that could write; they print only whether each variable is set).
- Phase 7b's scripts:
  - `npm run usage:report -- --live` (`--env production` after the "go", `--days N`; read-only, one MGET): the month's sessions (League page builds answered) against FantasyCalc's 10,000 with a warning from 8,000, today's work units against the day's work budget and its current slice, today's outbound requests against each platform's day, the capacity refusals, the month to date and a line a day. Vercel's own usage isn't in the store: `npx --yes vercel@62.2.0 usage --group-by project --non-interactive`, run from a folder other than this one.
  - `npm run snapshot:pull -- --live` (production by default, `--env preview`): copies the cron's archive (Sections 2.3, 2.9) into `data-local/` (projection weeks merged, each day's list kept as first written, and since Checkpoint 7c each day's levels not on disk yet into `data-local/projection-levels/`, one MGET for each 50 days, the day's first record kept), every record checked before it's written (a level's season and day are its key's); running it again writes only what's new.
  - `npm run feedback:report -- --live` (`--env production`, `--days N`): the anonymous feedback in totals only (Section 11).
  - `npm run load:test` (B4; offline by construction, Section 0.9; about 4.5 minutes): 50 requests at once through the real route code (withRoute, the handlers, adapters, crosswalk and model build over the real HTTP client) against mocked platforms and FakeRedis, scenarios 1–6 with multi-instance variants (`--scenarios`, `--redis-ms`, `--build-slots`, `--imports`, `--spread`, `--seed`); results and the fixes it led to in `reports/load-test.md`. The small live test the planner asked for ran on 2026-10-06 on the protected preview (5 imports and 5 League page builds of the public test league 899513 through the server path, all 200; `reports/load-test.md`, last section; ⚠️ ESPN requests per burst weren't measured).
  - `npm run vercel:secret -- --live --name CRON_SECRET` gives production its cron secret (CRON_SECRET's default environment is production, since Vercel runs crons there only; never shown).
  - The health check on a protected preview: `npx --yes vercel@62.2.0 curl <preview address>/api/health -- -s` (the logged-in owner's automatic bypass of Deployment Protection; don't pass `--non-interactive` there, it goes to curl).
- `npm run preview:deploy -- --live` (Checkpoint 7a; `scripts/preview-deploy.ts`) makes a **protected preview** deployment and nothing else: it refuses production options, checks `.vercelignore` is the allowlist, dry-runs the Vercel CLI to see every file that would upload (nothing private, under 2,000 files and 50 MB, framework nextjs), checks that Deployment Protection is on, deploys with `--project offersheet` (never `vercel link`, which writes a token into .env.local, seen at Checkpoint 7a), and removes the deployment at once if its address answers an anonymous visitor. Final review SEC-8, SEC-9: it also refuses any file to upload that isn't committed as it is (untracked, or changed since the last commit), runs the leak scan over exactly the files it would upload, and checks (by name only) that the Preview environment has SESSION_SECRET. `npm run vercel:secret -- --live` gives an environment its own random SESSION_SECRET (piped to `vercel env add … --sensitive`, never printed or kept; `--env production` at Checkpoint 7b; `--replace` resets that environment's sessions, tokens, cache keys and counts). Since Checkpoint 7c the CLI wrapper and the upload checks live in `scripts/lib/vercelCli.ts` (preview-deploy.ts re-exports them).
- `npm run prod:deploy` (Checkpoint 7c; planner reply to Checkpoint 7b, §2; `scripts/prod-deploy.ts`, `scripts/lib/cleanCopy.ts`) is production's only deploy path (Section 19).
  - Modes: no flags is a dry run on the newest checkpoint tag (every check, nothing uploaded; `--checkpoint <tag>` names the tag; `--ref <commit>` rehearses a commit that isn't tagged and can never deploy); `--offline` runs the local checks only; `--live --checkpoint <tag>` deploys; `--verify --checkpoint <tag>` runs the checks of the production domain again against the tag it serves (no Vercel CLI, no deploy, no wait: `/api/health` asked once as the health check, besides the cross-site rule's asks; review of the deploy track, deploy-4): the way to check again when a check after the deploy failed or the domain was slow to switch, never a second `--live` (a second production deploy leaves Hobby's Instant Rollback no way back to the landing page). Refused: a dropped `--` (npm's `npm_config_live`, `_checkpoint`, `_ref`, `_verify`, or a stray word), `--tag`, `--dry-run`, anything that reads like prod, promote, public or target, `--live` without `--checkpoint`, `--verify` with anything but `--checkpoint`.
  - Before any upload: the tag is annotated and the newest checkpoint by commit time (another tag on the same commit is the same code); the clean copy (the commit's own `.vercelignore` must be the allowlist, `!/<name>` rules only; its files exported with one `git cat-file --batch` into a temp folder, never a worktree or the working tree, so no core.autocrlf conversion; every file re-hashed to its blob id and nothing else in the folder; removed at the end, whatever happens), with the build's files and `vercel.json`'s framework, region and cron; what the checks after the deploy expect, from the tag itself, since the development folder may have moved past it (deploy-3): the tag's own Content-Security-Policy (the copy's `next.config.ts`, loaded under tsx and asked for `contentSecurityPolicy({ vercelPreview: false })`; its one import is a type) and the pages of `APP_PAGES` the tag has (one the tag lacks, or a page of the tag's the list doesn't name, is a ⚠️ and isn't asked); Vercel's own dry run in the copy (exactly the copy's files, plus the preview's upload checks); the leak check over the copy (`check-leaks --files <list> --base <copy>`, started in the main folder so its needles are the main folder's); Deployment Protection Standard (either name Vercel's API has for it, `all_except_custom_domains` or `prod_deployment_urls_and_all_previews`, and the line shows the one read; off or `all` fail, another name only warns since the domain is asked anyway; deploy-6); Production's variables by name only (`SESSION_SECRET`, `CRON_SECRET` and the store's URL and token required; Parker's ESPN cookies and league id, the development switches, the read-only store names and Vercel's own names refused; the Marketplace's extras noted as not read; `CONTACT_EMAIL` is a constant in `src/lib/site.ts`; `env ls`'s JSON never printed); the production domain answering anonymously; `/_next/image` and `/_vercel/image` already answering 403 there (the firewall rule is the project's, so a rule not yet published shows before anything uploads; deploy-4); since Checkpoint 7d the firewall's cross-site rule (planner reply to Checkpoint 7c, answer 4: "a request with that header gets 403, and the app still works"; Section 19): `/api/health` asked with `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` must get the firewall's 403, not the app's own BAD_ORIGIN 403 (`isAppCrossSiteRefusal`, by the app's JSON: that one got past the rate limit, which counted it), and with `same-origin` must not be denied (`crossSiteRuleProblems`; the line names the statuses and the rule only; ⚠️ the rules' order isn't asked: only past 80 requests could show it), and `/` opened from another site (`Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site`, as a followed link sends it) must not be denied either: a rule that lost its `/api/` path condition would deny every page reached from another site, which the `/api/` asks alone can't tell from the right rule (`crossSitePageProblems`; review of the policy-deploy track, PD-1); the rollback target (`vercel api /v9/projects/offersheet`, `targets.production.url`, the host only, never the production domain itself, which after the deploy serves the new deployment: a ⚠️ then, deploy-2; warn only, and ❌ if `autoExposeSystemEnvs` is false); a newer Next 16.3 patch on npm (warn only). A dry run lists every problem; a live run uploads only when none failed.
  - Live: `vercel deploy --prod --project offersheet --format json --meta offersheetCheckpoint=<tag> --meta offersheetCommit=<sha>`, run in the copy; every CLI call goes through one guard (`allowedVercelCall`: exactly the script's own calls, a deploy with `--dry`, or `--prod` once and live only, `--project offersheet`, at most `--format json` and the two `--meta`, and `project protection offersheet`, `env ls production --project offersheet --format json` and `api /v9/projects/offersheet` as written; any other flag, a `--flag=value` or single-dash form, another project or a path to deploy is refused, deploy-5; never link, pull, promote, alias, redeploy, rollback or remove), and every call runs in the copy, so whatever the CLI might write lands in a folder that's deleted. Then on https://offersheet.vercel.app: `/api/health` (up to 6 tries, 10 s apart, until the domain serves the new deployment) shows production, the shared store and its check (region iad1 warn only); the app pages the tag has answer 200 with the tag's own Content-Security-Policy, no report-only copy, the footer's FantasyCalc link and nothing loaded from another site (`foreignResourcesInHtml`); robots.txt names the production sitemap; the cron answers a bare 401 without the secret; `/_next/image` and `/_vercel/image` answer 403; the cross-site rule's three asks again (since Checkpoint 7d; the same-origin one now the health check's 200 and the page the app's 200), as `--verify` asks them; the deployment's own address asks for a login (warn only: a production deployment is never removed). `.env.local`'s sha256 is taken before the first CLI call and after the last (never printed; a change fails the run). Every live run, and a dry run's summary, ends with the rollback steps (`rollbackSteps`); a live run whose checks after the deploy failed also names the `--verify` command.
  - Tested offline with fakes (`scripts/prod-deploy.test.ts`: a dry run never deploys without `--dry`; a live run deploys exactly once, last, from the copy; any failed check blocks it, each named on its own line (the variables, an older or missing tag, the leak check, protection, the domain asking for a login, Vercel's dry run listing something private, leaving a file out or failing, a copy that isn't the commit byte for byte, `vercel.json` without the cron, the build's files missing, `autoExposeSystemEnvs` false, the image optimizer open, the tag's policy unknown, and since Checkpoint 7d the cross-site rule missing, denying the app's own requests, or without its `/api/` path condition, so denying pages opened from another site; deploy-1, checked by turning each such check into a warning in turn and seeing a test fail); the fake firewall answers in front of every deployment, as Vercel's does, and the fake app behind it with its own BAD_ORIGIN, which a test reads from the real `withRoute`; the checks after the deploy follow the tag's policy and pages; `--verify` makes no CLI call; the copy is removed; the rollback steps print; no forbidden call exists in the code; every run loads the copy's `next.config.ts` as the script does) and on a real throwaway repository (`scripts/lib/cleanCopy.test.ts`). `npm run prod:deploy -- --offline --ref HEAD` on `dfa4f5c`: 440 files, 8.7 MB, every blob id checked, the tag's own policy and its 10 app pages read, no hard leak hit, about 13 s; the same on `checkpoint-7b`. Seen live at Checkpoint 7c's dry run (`--ref HEAD` on 86f94d9, `reports/checkpoint-7c.md`): `deploy --dry` with `--meta`, `vercel api` with `--non-interactive` and the project JSON's `targets.production.url` (the rollback target read), and Standard Protection as `all_except_custom_domains`. ⚠️ Not yet seen live: the JSON of `deploy --prod --format json`, the cross-site rule's 403 (Checkpoint 7d), and `vercel rollback` run from a folder that isn't linked (the dashboard is the first way back). ⚠️ Like `stable.ts`, the script runs from the development folder, not from the tag: its checks, `APP_PAGES` and its Vercel calls are the folder's, and only the policy and the pages it compares production with come from the tag; it's reviewed with Checkpoint 7d and shouldn't change between the dry run and the go. A launch on `checkpoint-7c` (7d not approved in time) runs this same development-folder script, whose changes since Checkpoint 7c are the cross-site check (planner reply to Checkpoint 7c, answer 4); the commands its messages and usage spell out, which name `<the approved tag>` and, for `--verify`, `<the tag production serves>` instead of `checkpoint-7c` (`LIVE_COMMAND`, `VERIFY_COMMAND`; review of Checkpoint 7d, PD7D-5: `--verify` checks production against any tag it's given, so a fixed one copied after a launch on another tag would check the wrong one); and a firewall check that gets no answer at all, which fails as unconfirmed instead of asking for the rule, whose Deny always answers 403 (PD7D-4); its FantasyCalc link reader, shared with check:browser, was reworked for 7d's DynastyProcess check and reads the same.
- `npm run check:leaks -- --files <list> --base <dir>` (Checkpoint 7c): the listed files are read from `<dir>` while the needles still come from the folder it runs in; a `--files` scan with no `.env.local` value to look for is refused (the wrong folder), and in a `--files` scan a listed file it can't read is a hard failure. `scripts/check-leaks.test.ts` runs it on a made-up project folder.
- `npm test`, `npm run typecheck` and `npm run lint` must all pass before every checkpoint.

---

## 17. Phases

### Phase 0 — Data access gate (no app features)

1. **Scaffold** Next.js, TypeScript, Tailwind, Vitest and zod.
   - `.gitignore` must cover `.env*` (but keep `.env.example`), `.cache/` and `fixtures/raw/`.
   - Create `.env.example`.
   - Add at least one smoke test so `npm test` passes. Vitest exits with an error when there are no tests.
   - Run `git init` and make the first commit.

2. **Write probe scripts:**
   - `scripts/probe-espn.ts`
   - `scripts/probe-sleeper.ts`
   - `scripts/probe-shared.ts`: FantasyCalc, the ID map, the NFL schedule, and Sleeper and ESPN projections
   - `scripts/probe-fleaflicker.ts`
   - `scripts/probe-mfl.ts`
   - `scripts/probe-yahoo.ts`: only if the `YAHOO_*` variables are set; otherwise report "awaiting Yahoo approval"
   - Add `npm run probe:all`.

3. **Pick test leagues.** Use Parker's leagues from `.env.local` first. For any platform where he has no league:
   - **ESPN:** public league **899513**, season 2026. (Phase 0 found it has drafted, so it also serves the scoring oracle.)
   - **Sleeper:** ask Parker for any league ID or username.
   - **MFL:** find one with `leagueSearch`.
   - **Fleaflicker:** ask Parker, or try a known public league.
   - **Fantrax:** skip it unless someone has a public league ID.

4. **Check every item** in the Data Checklist below, for each platform. Print ✅, ⚠️ or ❌ with the JSON path and a sample value. Include no secrets and no leaguemates' names.

5. **Save** raw responses to `fixtures/raw/` and sanitized copies to `fixtures/sanitized/`.

6. **Write** `reports/data-access.md` (the platform × checklist matrix) and the Checkpoint 0 report. Then **stop**.

**Data Checklist**

| | Item | What to confirm |
|---|---|---|
| A | League basics | Season, current week, number of teams, regular-season weeks, playoff start week, number of playoff teams and byes, trade deadline, trade review/veto setting, league type (redraft/keeper/dynasty), median-game and points-only flags, divisions |
| B | Lineup | Every starting slot and its count, including FLEX variants, SUPER_FLEX/OP, K, DEF and IDP; bench, IR and taxi sizes; position limits; max roster size |
| C | Scoring | Every rule and its points: receptions (including position-specific), pass/rush/receiving yards and TDs, interceptions, fumbles, 2-point conversions, first downs, bonuses, kickers, DEF/DST and IDP. List anything unmapped. |
| D | Teams | IDs, names, owner display names, and which team is Parker's |
| E | Rosters | Every player on every team, including IR and taxi; position; NFL team; injury status; platform player ID |
| F | Schedule | Fantasy matchups for every remaining regular-season week; current W-L-T and points for/against |
| G | Transactions | This season's trades, with week or date and the players involved; add/drop counts per team |
| H | Free agents | Available players, either listed or derivable |
| I | Draft picks | Future pick ownership, for dynasty and keeper leagues (Sleeper `traded_picks`, MFL `futureDraftPicks`) |
| J | Projections | Current-week and future-week projections for each player, either league-scored or stat-level |
| K | Completed weeks | Actual stats plus league-scored points, for the scoring oracle |
| L | NFL schedule | Bye weeks and the NFL schedule |
| M | FantasyCalc match rate | The share of rostered QB/RB/WR/TE matched to FantasyCalc. Target: at least 98%. List the misses. |

### Phase 1 — ESPN and Sleeper import, ID crosswalk, scoring engine, Import Report

**Build**
- adapters that produce a `LeagueSnapshot`
- the player ID crosswalk (DynastyProcess map plus FantasyCalc IDs), so every player has a canonical ID from the start
- the scoring maps and oracle tests
- the Connect and Import Report pages
- "my team" detection
- ESPN credentials entered on the Connect page:
  - send them to the server in a POST body
  - keep them only in an encrypted, httpOnly, **session-only** cookie (no Expires or Max-Age)
  - never put them in a URL, localStorage or logs
  - Parker's own credentials can still come from `.env.local` in development, only with `USE_ENV_COOKIES=1` (Section 0.5)
- the ESPN connect flow from Section 14 (public link first; for private leagues, ask the commissioner or use cookies)
- the general scoring rules (Sections 4 and 5) and the model fields added after Checkpoint 0 (`P`, starter limits, roster minimums, best ball, playoff seeding)
- the Sleeper decisions from Section 2.3 (trade review, position limits, guillotine rejection, best-ball note)

**Checkpoint 1 includes**
- the Import Report contents for Parker's leagues, described in words
- the oracle results, per rule type
- all warnings
- every CLAUDE.md section changed since Checkpoint 0

### Phase 2 — Projections, lineup solver, market values, needs, League page

**Build**
- the projection model, including future injury risk (Section 6, step 3)
- rest-of-season points shown to users (Section 6, step 7)
- the lineup solver, with availability scenarios for exact evaluation (Section 7)
- FantasyCalc values
- needs, surplus and best trade partners
- the League page, without playoff odds (those arrive in Phase 3)
- the FantasyCalc format note in the Import Report (Section 14), deferred from Phase 1
- the Checkpoint 1 answers: count-stat distributions (Section 5), the PROBABLE status, the `USE_ENV_COOKIES` development default, the crosswalk rules (Section 2.9) and the compact phone picker (Section 14)

**Checkpoint 2 includes**
- Parker's projected points per week against the rest of the league
- ROS points and projected points per week for every rostered player in Parker's league
- the needs and surplus table for every team
- the FantasyCalc has-a-value and ID match rates
- lineup solver test results
- timings, including the runtime impact of the availability scenarios
- a before/after example of a bench player's value to his team under expected-value lineups versus scenario lineups
- every CLAUDE.md section changed since Checkpoint 1

### Phase 3 — Season simulator

**Build**
- the season simulator, with the weekly team σ adding the spread across availability scenarios (Section 8), and the league's own seeding tiebreaker when the platform reports it
- playoff, bye and title odds on the League page, and each remaining week's risk (Section 8)
- the Checkpoint 2 answers:
  - free-agent-adjusted baselines for MV_add as well as MV_lose (Section 7)
  - trade chips in place of surplus, and partners built from them (Section 7)
  - Sleeper as the tiebreaker for a far ESPN return date on a Q/D/P player, with the note (Section 6)
  - pre-game projection snapshots and `npm run snapshot:projections` (Section 2.3)
  - per-week projection caching and single-flight fetches (Section 2.10)

**Checkpoint 3 includes**
- playoff, bye and title odds for all teams in Parker's league, next to the current standings
- simulation runtime at 1,000 and at 5,000 simulations
- the no-op check: a trade that changes nothing moves no odds
- a Trade Score scale check: for Parker's team, B and the Trade Score from a hypothetical +1, +3 and +6 projected points per week for the rest of the season. The planner's toy league gave B ≈ 1.7 / 5 / 10. If Parker's league differs a lot, propose a new B_ref.
- every CLAUDE.md section changed since Checkpoint 2

### Phase 4 — Trade search, benefit, acceptance, Trade Finder and Analyzer

**Build** what Sections 10–13 specify, plus the Checkpoint 3 decisions:
- first, streaming positions (Section 7, model change 1), because they change baselines, needs, chips and B; then the free-agent optimizer (model change 2) everywhere a trade is scored
- B_ref 6, the Q/D/P base-rate rule (Section 6), and the chip display (cost and starter tag)
- the three numbers on every card, sortable, with Overall as the default sort; ROS points given and received, required drops and adds, risk flags, plain-English reasons and the copy-ready pitch message (Sections 10 and 13)
- the feedback buttons (Sent / Accepted / Rejected / Countered, and Report with its reasons), stored locally, with a JSON export and the summary script (Section 11)
- other teams' completed ESPN trades, which come without their players, refilled from player data, so the activity and hist features have data (approved at Checkpoint 1)
- the Trade Analyzer: the user builds any trade, including counteroffers, and sees the same evaluation
- the Settings page (Section 14 item 6), including "Positions I stream weekly"
- every trade chip joins the give pool; in a keeper league without dynasty values ΔDyn is missing and the card says so (Checkpoint 2)
- performance: 10 seconds or less for Parker's league, in the Web Worker, with a progress bar

**Checkpoint 4 includes**
- Parker's top 10 trades: for each, give/get, the three numbers, ΔPPW, ΔPO, ΔCH, ROS points in and out, g_mkt for both sides, the partner's n, the reasons and the pitch message
- a realism check on the top 25: the distribution of g_mkt(partner). If most suggestions ask partners to give up more than 15% in value, say so: the search would be favoring lopsided offers
- a consistency check: no suggested trade's B comes from free-agent pickups. Confirm it holds by construction, and show one test
- the acceptance anchor tests, still within ±1 point
- runtime, total and per stage (screening, exact evaluation, screening sim, final sim)
- one example evaluated in the Trade Analyzer: a trade that looks great for Parker but that the model gives a low acceptance chance, and why
- the free-agent optimizer: the Bo Nix → Team 1 example, McCaffrey's chip gap under the new baseline, and how many moves teams typically make
- every CLAUDE.md section changed since Checkpoint 3
- the planner's addendum (`reports/planner-reply-3b.md`), applied before the reports were regenerated: the star premium (§11), "Harder to get" (§11, §14), "Protect my trade value" (§10), and "Offer first / if they say no" (§13); and in the report: Parker's top 10 before vs after the addendum with the three numbers and any "Harder to get" players, the Smith-Njigba example under the old and new models, the g_mkt(me) distribution of the top 25 before and after, and how many cards show an "If they say no" step

### Phase 4b — The planner's reply to Checkpoint 4 (`reports/planner-reply-4.md`)

**Why:** the live results at Checkpoint 4 showed the model wasn't ready for Parker to act on. The top ideas mostly sold Garrett Wilson (15 of 22) and bought injury-tagged players (8 of the top 10): the search ranks thousands of trades by our own model's edge, so its top fills with the places our projections disagree most with the market (the optimizer's curse). And the acceptance estimate rated trades that cost the partner's lineup too high.

**Build**
- market-blended projections (Section 6 step 8)
- the acceptance rebalance: β_need 1.2 in redraft and keeper leagues, the 0.85 cap, the new anchor tables (Section 11)
- one free agent, one team, in waiver order (Section 7)
- the value floor at W on both sides, replacing lead decision N10 (Sections 9 and 10)
- IR players: their return counted in team strength, tradable, IR-slot legality (Sections 6, 7 and 12)
- cards and pages: injury tags, Plan B, at most 3 trades in the top 20 giving the same player of mine, "Best of their trade chips" and "See trade ideas with Team X →", and the version sent in the feedback (Sections 11, 13 and 14)
- `npm run check:browser` and the local FantasyCalc archive (Sections 2.9 and 16)
- the answers to Checkpoint 4's questions: κ stays Medium; the throw-in rules stay; N11 and N12 are confirmed; up to about 3 lineup points per player from pickups is accepted; draft picks wait for Phase 5; cold load times are accepted until Phase 7's shared cache; the review period goes to Phase 6; after 4b the order stays Phase 5 → 6 → 7

**Checkpoint 4b includes**
- Parker's top 10, before (Checkpoint 4) and after, with the three numbers, injury flags, how many receive an injury-tagged player and how many give Garrett Wilson
- blend sensitivity: the median g_mkt(you) of the top 10 and the top 25 at `marketBlend` 0, 0.25 and 0.5
- Parker's odds over time (Checkpoint 3: 78.6% / 19.7%; Checkpoint 4: 67.8% / 13.3% as rosters stand, 62.9% / 11.6% with pickups; now), each move explained by cause: roster and injury news, streaming, the pickup baseline, and model changes
- the free-agent allocation: how many duplicate pickups went away, where Mark Andrews lands, and whether any top trade still depends on a pickup another team would make first
- cards: how many show Plan B and how many an offer-first step
- the new anchor tables passing
- runtime, total and per stage
- every CLAUDE.md section changed since Checkpoint 4

**Why:** Checkpoint 4c is the first list Parker sends real offers from, and trade deadlines are in November. After launch: Phase 5, then Phase 6, then the Chrome extension.

**Part B, the beta (B1–B3; B4 is Checkpoint 7b)**
- B1 infrastructure: a shared server cache (Upstash Redis's free tier through the Vercel Marketplace, single-flight, hashed keys, private-league data keyed by a credential hash); per-IP rate limits on every server route; outbound pacing and a daily request budget per platform, especially ESPN; Sleeper and public ESPN leagues fetched from the user's browser (private ESPN stays on the server); friendly "the data source is down" messages; error logging that never records credentials or league data
- B2: the anonymized server-side feedback store, with an opt-out and rate limits (Section 11)
- B3: the home page with the landing page's wording and a "Beta" label, `/privacy` rewritten to match, `/terms`, the footer (a feedback link, the attributions, "Not affiliated with ESPN or Sleeper…"), a PWA manifest and icons, an SEO title, description and Open Graph image, everything at 375 px; the Connect page keeps both private-ESPN paths

**Part B as built** (Checkpoint 7a; two build waves of five tracks, each built, reviewed and fixed, then a final review)
- B1: the shared store and cache, the server routes' limits, budgets, deadlines and error log, and browser reads with signed imports (Section 3); the policy switches and the deploy script (Sections 14 and 16). Upstash is used on Vercel only; locally everything runs as before.
- B2: the anonymous feedback store (Section 11).
- B3: the pages, installable app, sharing image, search files, error pages and headers (Section 14).
- Deploying: `.vercelignore` (an allowlist: src, public and the build's config files), `vercel.json` (framework nextjs, iad1), `engines.node` "22.x" (the project defaulted to Node 24), `SESSION_SECRET` set for Preview (generated, never shown), and `npm run preview:deploy -- --live` (Section 16). The project `offersheet` has Fluid compute on (300 s default), Standard Protection with Vercel Authentication, and isn't connected to git.
